<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 3. LEGISLATURE</h2>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATURE</h1>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.3.01">
        <h1>3.01 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.01.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/1/#laws..3.0">1973 c 1 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.01.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/71/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 71 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.011">
        <h1 class="shn">3.011 SESSIONS.</h1>
        <p>The legislature shall meet at the seat of government on the first Tuesday after the second Monday in January of each odd-numbered year. It shall also meet when called by the governor to meet in special session.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.011.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/1/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.7.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.012">
        <h1 class="shn">3.012 LEGISLATIVE DAY.</h1>
        <p>For the purposes of the Minnesota Constitution, article IV, section 12, a legislative day is a day when either house of the legislature gives any bill a third reading, adopts a rule of procedure or organization, elects a university regent, confirms a gubernatorial appointment, or votes to override a gubernatorial veto. A legislative day begins at seven o'clock a.m. and continues until seven o'clock a.m. of the following calendar day.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.012.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/1/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.8.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.02">
        <h1 class="shn">3.02 EVIDENCE OF MEMBERSHIP.</h1>
        <p>For all purposes of organization of either house of the legislature, a certificate of election to it, duly executed by the secretary of state, is prima facie evidence of the right to membership of the person named in it.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.02.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">25</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/9/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 9 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/132/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 132 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.03"><b>3.03</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/561/#laws.0.17.0">1961 c 561 s 17</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.04"><b>3.04</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/561/#laws.0.17.0">1961 c 561 s 17</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.05">
        <h1 class="shn">3.05 ORGANIZATION.</h1>
        <p>At noon of the day appointed for convening the legislature, the members shall meet in their respective chambers. The lieutenant governor shall call the senate to order and the secretary of state, the house of representatives. In the absence of either officer, the oldest member present shall act in the officer's place. The person so acting shall appoint, from the members present, a clerk pro tem, who shall call the legislative districts in the order of their numbers. As each is called, the persons claiming to be members from each shall present their certificates to be filed. All whose certificates are so presented shall then stand and be sworn.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">28</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.055">
        <h1 class="shn">3.055 OPEN MEETINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.055.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Meetings to be open.</span></h2>
          <p>Meetings of the legislature shall be open to the public, including sessions of the senate, sessions of the house of representatives, joint sessions of the senate and the house of representatives, and meetings of a standing committee, committee division, subcommittee, conference committee, or legislative commission, but not including a caucus of the members of any of those bodies from the same house and political party nor a delegation of legislators representing a geographic area or political subdivision. For purposes of this section, a meeting occurs when a quorum is present and action is taken regarding a matter within the jurisdiction of the body. Each house shall provide by rule for posting notices of meetings, recording proceedings, and making the recordings and votes available to the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.055.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Meetings by interactive TV.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A meeting governed by this section may be conducted by interactive television so long as:</p>
          <p>(1) all members of the body participating in the meeting, wherever their physical location, can hear and see one another and can hear and see all discussion and testimony presented at any location at which at least one member is present;</p>
          <p>(2) members of the public present at the regular meeting location of the body can hear and see all discussion and testimony and all votes of members of the body; and</p>
          <p>(3) at least one member of the body is physically present at the regular meeting location.</p>
          <p>(b) Each member of a body participating in a meeting by interactive television is considered present at the meeting for purposes of determining a quorum and participating in all proceedings.</p>
          <p>(c) If interactive television is used to conduct a meeting, to the extent practical, a body shall allow a person to monitor the meeting electronically from a remote location. The body may require the person making such a connection to pay for documented marginal costs that the body incurs as a result of the additional connection.</p>
          <p>(d) House of representatives and senate rules governing notice of meetings must provide for giving notice that interactive television will be used to conduct a meeting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.055.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>The house of representatives and the senate shall adopt rules to implement this section. Remedies provided by rules of the house of representatives and senate are exclusive. No court or administrative agency has jurisdiction to enforce, enjoin, penalize, award damages, or otherwise act upon a violation or alleged violation of this section, to invalidate any provision of law because of a violation of this section, or to otherwise interpret this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.055.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.6.1.0">1990 c 608 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/370/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 370 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/154/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 154 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.056">
        <h1 class="shn">3.056 DESIGNATION OF SUCCESSOR COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <p>If a law assigns a power or duty to a named legislative committee or its chair, and the committee has been renamed or no longer exists, the speaker of the house or the senate Committee on Rules and Administration shall designate the successor committee or chair for the law as provided in this section. If the committee has been renamed but retains jurisdiction of the subject of the power or duty, the speaker or senate committee shall designate the renamed committee as successor. If the committee has been renamed and jurisdiction of the subject of the power or duty has been transferred to another committee, the speaker or senate committee shall designate the committee with current jurisdiction as the successor. If the named committee no longer exists, the speaker or senate committee shall designate as successor the committee with the jurisdiction that most closely corresponds with the former jurisdiction of the named committee. The house of representatives and the senate shall maintain a list on the World Wide Web of renamed or successor committees to committees that are referenced in law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.056.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/4/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.2.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.06">
        <h1 class="shn">3.06 OFFICERS AND EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.06.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Election.</span></h2>
          <p>Thereupon, if a quorum is present, the houses shall elect the following officers, any of whom may be removed by resolution of the appointing body.</p>
          <p>The senate shall elect a secretary, a first and a second assistant secretary, an enrolling clerk, an engrossing clerk, a sergeant-at-arms, an assistant sergeant-at-arms, and a chaplain.</p>
          <p>The house of representatives shall elect a speaker, who shall be a member of the house of representatives, a chief clerk, a first and a second assistant clerk, an index clerk, a chief sergeant-at-arms, a first and a second assistant sergeant-at-arms, a postmaster, an assistant postmaster, and a chaplain.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.06.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Successors.</span></h2>
          <p>If an officer of the house of representatives or senate resigns or dies, the duties of the officer shall be performed by a successor as provided in the rules of the officer's house until a successor is elected at a regular or special session.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">29</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">30</a>) GS 1894 s 220; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1905/0/52/#laws.0.1.0">1905 c 52 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1936/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">Ex1936 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/233/#laws.0.1.0">1947 c 233 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/1/2/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp1987 c 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.07">
        <h1 class="shn">3.07 ADDITIONAL EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <p>Each house, after its organization, may appoint and at pleasure remove the employees provided for by its permanent rules or recommended by its Committee on Rules. All officers and employees shall receive the compensation provided by the permanent rules of the electing or appointing body or recommended by its Committee on Rules. Unless otherwise expressly provided by law, no officer or employee shall receive any other compensation for services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.07.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">31</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/233/#laws.0.2.0">1947 c 233 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/457/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 457 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.073">
        <h1 class="shn">3.073 ORGANIZATION OF SPECIAL SESSION.</h1>
        <p>The officers elected, the rules adopted, and the committees established by the legislature and by each house during the preceding regular session shall serve and be in effect during a special session, except as the legislature or a house provides otherwise.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.073.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/566/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 566 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.08">
        <h1 class="shn">3.08 ELECTION; DUTIES.</h1>
        <p>In addition to the duties prescribed by law, the officers and employees shall perform the services required of them by rule or vote of the appointing body or by direction of a committee of the appointing body.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">32</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/233/#laws.0.3.0">1947 c 233 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.081"><b>3.081</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/286/#laws.0.21.0">1977 c 286 s 21</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.082">
        <h1 class="shn">3.082 MEMBERS' EMPLOYMENT; CONTINUATION.</h1>
        <p>A member of the legislature of the state of Minnesota who held a position, other than a temporary position, in the employ of a private employer in Minnesota at the commencement of service in a legislative session, who applies for reemployment not later than 30 days after the last legislative day in each calendar year, shall be continued in or restored to the position, or to a position of like seniority, status and pay. Retirement benefits under an employer-sponsored pension or retirement plan shall not be reduced because of time spent in legislative service.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.082.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/306/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 306 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/574/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 574 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.083">
        <h1 class="shn">3.083 RETENTION OF SENIORITY, FRINGE BENEFITS AND TENURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.083.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Entitlement to benefits and position.</span></h2>
          <p>A member of the legislature who is continued in or restored to a position in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.082">3.082</a>:</p>
          <p>(1) shall be continued or restored without loss of seniority;</p>
          <p>(2) may participate in insurance or other benefits offered by the employer under its established rules and practices; and</p>
          <p>(3) shall not be discharged without good cause from the position for three years after the continuation or restoration except in the reverse order of seniority with the employer within the field of the legislator's training and experience.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.083.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">No employer discrimination.</span></h2>
          <p>No employer or employee organization may discharge or otherwise discriminate against an employee or member who is or was a member of the legislature in retribution for statements made or beliefs held by the employee or member in the capacity as a member of the legislature. For purposes of this subdivision, "employee organization" means a union or organization of employees which exists, in whole or in part, for collective bargaining or dealing with employers concerning grievances or term or conditions of employment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.083.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/306/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 306 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/650/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 650 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.084">
        <h1 class="shn">3.084 LOBBYING ACTIVITIES PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.084.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this section, "lobbying" means engaging in activities that would require an individual to register as a lobbyist, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.01">10A.01</a>, subdivision 21.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.084.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A sitting member of the legislature is prohibited from accepting employment with or otherwise receiving compensation for services performed from:</p>
          <p>(1) a business whose primary source of revenue is derived from lobbying, government relations or government affairs services;</p>
          <p>(2) a business whose primary source of revenue is derived from facilitating government relations or government affairs services between two third parties; or</p>
          <p>(3) any other business that employs or contracts with lobbyists, government relations or government affairs professionals, if the member's job duties include acting in that capacity or providing direct or indirect consulting, advice, or administrative support for that work.</p>
          <p>(b) This prohibition applies regardless of the location where the work of the business is substantially conducted or its clients are located.</p>
          <p>(c) The house of representatives and the senate must adopt rules to enforce this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.084.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.11.1.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 11 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.085"><b>3.085</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/306/#laws.0.5.0">1974 c 306 s 5</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.086"><b>3.086</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/306/#laws.0.5.0">1974 c 306 s 5</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.087">
        <h1 class="shn">3.087 RIGHT OF ACTION IN DISTRICT COURT.</h1>
        <p>If a private employer fails or refuses to comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.082">3.082</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.083">3.083</a>, the district court where the private employer maintains a place of business may, upon the filing of a complaint by the member entitled to the benefits of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.082">3.082</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.083">3.083</a>, specifically require the employer to comply with their provisions and compensate the member for any loss of wages or benefits suffered by reason of the employer's unlawful action. The court shall order a speedy hearing in the case and advance it on the calendar.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.087.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/690/#laws.0.3.0">1955 c 690 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/306/#laws.0.3.0">1974 c 306 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.088">
        <h1 class="shn">3.088 LEAVE OF ABSENCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.088.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Leave of absence without pay.</span></h2>
          <p>Subject to this section, any appointed officer or employee of a political subdivision, municipal corporation, or school district of the state or an institution of learning maintained by the state who serves as a legislator or is elected to a full-time city or county office or to an Indian tribal council in Minnesota is entitled to a leave of absence from the public office or to employment without pay when on the business of the office, with right of reinstatement as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.088.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reinstatement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in this section, upon the completion of the last legislative day in each calendar year, or, in the case of an elected city, county, or tribal council official, on the completion of the final day of the term to which the official was elected, the officer or employee shall be reinstated in the public position held at the time of entry into the legislature or taking city, county, or tribal council office, or be placed in a public position of like seniority, status, and pay if it is available at the same salary which would have been received if the leave had not been taken, upon the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) that the position has not been abolished or that its term, if limited, has not expired;</p>
          <p>(2) that the legislator makes a written application for reinstatement to the appointing authority within 30 days after the last legislative day in a calendar year or, in the case of an elected city, county, or tribal council official, within 30 days after the expiration of the elected term; and</p>
          <p>(3) that the request for reinstatement is made not later than ten years after the granting of the leave.</p>
          <p class="bl">Upon reinstatement, the officer or employee shall have the same rights with respect to accrued and future seniority status, efficiency rating, vacation, insurance benefits, sick leave, and other benefits as if actually employed during the time of the leave. No public employer is required to compensate a reinstated employee or officer for time spent by that employee or officer away from work for the employer and on the business of the state legislature during the period between the first and last legislative day in each calendar year or on the business of an elected city, county, or tribal council office. No officer or employee reinstated shall be removed or discharged within one year after reinstatement except for cause and after notice and hearing, but this does not extend a term of service limited by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.088.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Pension and retirement rights.</span></h2>
          <p>A public officer or employee who receives leave of absence under this section or is elected as a state constitutional officer and has rights in a state, municipal, or other public pension, retirement, or relief system shall retain all the rights accrued up to the time of taking leave. Time spent by the employee as a member of the legislature or as an elected city, county, or tribal council official or state constitutional officer shall be calculated in the same manner as if the employee had spent that time in the service of the public employer for the purpose of determining vesting of the employee's rights in the employer's pension, retirement, or relief system. Under no circumstances shall two governmental units pay the employee's share of pension contributions when the employee is on leave of absence to serve in the legislature or as an elected city, county, or tribal council official.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.088.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Vacancies to be filled temporarily.</span></h2>
          <p>When a public officer or employee is absent with leave under this section and it is necessary to provide for the performance of the duties of the absentee's position during the absence, the authority having power to fill a vacancy in the position may appoint an acting incumbent, who shall qualify as required for the regular incumbent, receive the same compensation as fixed by law or proper authority, and have the powers and perform the duties of the position until the return of the regular incumbent. This section does not preclude making other lawful provision for the discharge of the duties of the position.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.088.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Supplementary.</span></h2>
          <p>The rights and privileges granted by this section do not apply if the elected office is constitutionally or legally incompatible with the public office or employment or the elected person chooses to take leave as provided by other law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.088.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Pensions.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law or ordinance or state, municipal, or other public retirement or relief association rule or bylaw, a person shall not be disqualified from receiving a legislative retirement pension or allowance because the person is entitled to receive a public pension or retirement benefit as a result of employment by another public employer. The person shall receive both the legislative retirement pension or allowance and any state, municipal, or other public pension or retirement benefit for which the person has qualified.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.088.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/306/#laws.0.4.0">1974 c 306 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/140/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 140 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/308/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 308 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/92/#laws.1.1.0">2017 c 92 art 1 s 1</a>-3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.09">
        <h1 class="shn">3.09 COMPENSATION OF EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <p>The compensation of officers and employees shall be at the rates fixed by the permanent rules of the electing or appointing body or recommended by its Committee on Rules.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">33</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/229/#laws.0.1.0">1907 c 229 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1909/0/132/#laws.0.1.0">1909 c 132 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1936/1/115/#laws.0.1.0">Ex1936 c 115 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/1/82/#laws.0.1.0">Ex1937 c 82 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/233/#laws.0.5.0">1947 c 233 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/457/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 457 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.095">
        <h1 class="shn">3.095 LEGISLATIVE EMPLOYEES, LEAVES.</h1>
        <p>The Legislative Coordinating Commission shall adopt plans for sick leave and annual leave for the employees of the legislature and of legislative committees and commissions.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.095.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/901/#laws.0.76.0">1965 c 901 s 76</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/507/#laws.0.45.0">1973 c 507 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/617/#laws.0.47.0">1980 c 617 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/210/#laws.0.47.0">1981 c 210 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/457/#laws.0.3.0">2000 c 457 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.096">
        <h1 class="shn">3.096 TRANSFER OF LEAVE.</h1>
        <p>An employee in the classified or unclassified service who accepts a position as an employee of the legislature shall have accrued vacation and sick leave transferred and placed to the employee's credit on the legislative records. An employee of the legislature who accepts a position in the classified or unclassified service shall have accrued vacation and sick leave transferred and placed to the employee's credit on the records of the new appointing authority. Vacation and sick leave are not transferred if the new position does not provide for the leave. The amount of vacation and sick leave that may be transferred is subject to any limitations imposed by the receiving agency's collective bargaining agreement or compensation plan.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.096.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/48/#laws.0.65.0">Ex1967 c 48 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/221/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 221 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/457/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 457 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.098">
        <h1 class="shn">3.098 EXPENSE REPORTS.</h1>
        <p>The house of representatives and senate shall by rule require detailed quarterly reports of expenditures by the house of representatives and senate to their respective committees on rules and legislative administration. These reports are public information.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.098.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/370/#laws.0.9.0">1993 c 370 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/99/#laws.0.6.0">1999 c 99 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.099">
        <h1 class="shn">3.099 MEMBERS; COMPENSATION AND EXPENSES, FLEXIBLE SESSIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.099.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Paydays; mileage; per diem.</span></h2>
          <p>The compensation of each member of the legislature is due on the first day of the regular legislative session of the term and payable in equal parts on January 15, in the first month of each term and on the first day of each following month during the term for which the member was elected. The compensation of each member of the legislature elected at a special election is due on the day the member takes the oath of office and payable within ten days of taking the oath for the remaining part of the month in which the oath was taken, and then in equal parts on the first day of each following month during the term for which the member was elected.</p>
          <p>Each member shall receive mileage for necessary travel to the place of meeting and returning to the member's residence in the amount and for trips as authorized by the senate for senate members and by the house of representatives for house members.</p>
          <p>Each member shall also receive per diem living expenses during a regular or special session of the legislature in the amounts and for the purposes as determined by the senate for senate members and by the house of representatives for house members.</p>
          <p>On January 15 in the first month of each term and on the first day of each following month, the secretary of the senate and the chief clerk of the house of representatives shall certify to the commissioner of management and budget, in duplicate, the amount of compensation then payable to each member of their respective houses and its total.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.099.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.191.0">1987 c 404 s 191</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.099.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Leaders.</span></h2>
          <p>The senate Committee on Rules and Administration for the senate and the house of representatives Committee on Rules and Legislative Administration for the house of representatives may each designate for their respective body up to five leadership positions to receive up to 140 percent of the compensation of other members.</p>
          <p>At the commencement of each biennial legislative session, each house of the legislature shall adopt a resolution designating its majority and minority leader.</p>
          <p>The majority leader is the person elected by the caucus of members in each house which is its largest political affiliation. The minority leader is the person elected by the caucus which is its second largest political affiliation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.099.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/32/#laws.0.22.0">Ex1971 c 32 s 22</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/35/#laws.0.10.0">1977 c 35 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.30.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.59.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.9.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.10"><b>3.10</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/32/#laws.0.22.0">Ex1971 c 32 s 22</a> subd 2]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.101">
        <h1 class="shn">3.101 LIVING EXPENSES.</h1>
        <p>A member of the legislature in addition to the compensation and mileage otherwise provided by law shall be reimbursed for living and other expenses incurred in the performance of duties or engaging in official business during a regular or special session and when the legislature is not in session in the manner and amount prescribed by the senate Committee on Rules and Administration for senators and by the house of representatives Committee on Rules and Legislative Administration for house of representatives members.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1139/#laws.0.70.0">1969 c 1139 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/648/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 648 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.102"><b>3.102</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/648/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 648 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.103">
        <h1 class="shn">3.103 SPECIAL SESSION LIVING EXPENSES.</h1>
        <p>Each member of the legislature, during a special session, shall be reimbursed for expenses incurred in the performance of duties in the same amounts, for the same purposes, and in the same manner as authorized for senators and members of the house of representatives at the last regular session before the special session. Reimbursement for travel shall not exceed one round trip per member for each seven calendar days in which the legislature meets in the special session.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.103.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/3/#laws.0.70.0">Ex1971 c 3 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.11"><b>3.11</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/811/#laws.0.2.0">1957 c 811 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.12"><b>3.12</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/561/#laws.0.17.0">1961 c 561 s 17</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.13"><b>3.13</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/35/#laws.0.21.0">1977 c 35 s 21</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.14">
        <h1 class="shn">3.14 CONTEMPTS.</h1>
        <p>Each house may punish, as a contempt, a breach of its privileges, or of the privileges of its members, but only for the following offenses:</p>
        <p>(1) arresting or causing to be arrested, a member or officer in violation of the member's privilege from arrest;</p>
        <p>(2) disorderly conduct in its view and presence, or in the view and presence of any of its committees, tending to interrupt its proceedings;</p>
        <p>(3) giving or offering a bribe to a member, or attempting by menace or corrupt or improper means, directly or indirectly, to control or influence a member in giving or withholding the member's vote.</p>
        <p>No person shall be excused from attending and testifying before either house of the legislature, or a committee of either house, for an alleged offense upon an investigation of giving or offering a bribe, or attempting by menace or corrupt or improper means, directly or indirectly, to control or influence a member in giving or withholding the member's vote upon the ground that the person's required testimony or evidence, documentary or otherwise, may tend to convict the person of a crime or subject the person to a penalty. No person shall be prosecuted, or subjected to a penalty for a transaction, matter, or thing concerning which the person may so testify, or produce evidence, documentary or otherwise. No testimony, so given or produced, shall be received against the person in any criminal investigation or proceeding.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.14.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">38</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/319/#laws.0.1.0">1907 c 319 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/227/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 227 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.15">
        <h1 class="shn">3.15 PUNISHMENT FOR CONTEMPT.</h1>
        <p>Punishment for contempt shall be by imprisonment. The term of imprisonment shall not extend beyond the session at which it is inflicted. When either house shall direct the imprisonment of a person for a contempt the keeper of the jail of the county in which the seat of government is situated shall receive and detain the person in close confinement during the term fixed by the order of commitment, or until the detainee is discharged by vote of the committing body or due process of law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">39</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.151">
        <h1 class="shn">3.151 DISTURBING LEGISLATURE OR INTIMIDATING MEMBER.</h1>
        <p>A person is guilty of a gross misdemeanor who:</p>
        <p>(1) willfully disturbs the legislature, or either house of it, while in session;</p>
        <p>(2) commits disorderly conduct in the presence and view of either house, tending to interrupt its proceedings or impair the respect due to its authority; or</p>
        <p>(3) willfully, by intimidation or otherwise, prevents a member of the legislature from attending a session of the member's house, or of a committee of it, or from giving the member's vote upon a question which may come before the house, or from performing any other official act.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.151.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/96">10000</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/96">RL s 4815</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.152"><b>3.152</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/227/#laws.0.3.0">1971 c 227 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.153">
        <h1 class="shn">3.153 LEGISLATIVE SUBPOENAS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.153.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissions; committees.</span></h2>
          <p>A joint legislative commission established by law and composed exclusively of legislators or a standing or interim legislative committee, by a two-thirds vote of its members, may request the issuance of subpoenas, including subpoenas duces tecum, requiring the appearance of persons, production of relevant records, and the giving of relevant testimony. Subpoenas shall be issued by the chief clerk of the house of representatives or the secretary of the senate upon receipt of the request. A person subpoenaed to attend a meeting of the legislature or a hearing of a legislative committee or commission shall receive the same fees and expenses provided by law for witnesses in district court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.153.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Service.</span></h2>
          <p>Service of a subpoena authorized by this section shall be made in the manner provided for the service of subpoenas in civil actions at least seven days before the date fixed in the subpoena for appearance or production of records unless a shorter period is authorized by a majority vote of all the members of the committee or commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.153.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Counsel.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person served with a subpoena may choose to be accompanied by counsel if a personal appearance is required and shall be served with a notice to that effect. The person shall also be served with a copy of the resolution or statute establishing the committee or commission and a general statement of the subject matter of the commission or committee's investigation or inquiry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.153.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Attachment.</span></h2>
          <p>To carry out the authority granted by this section, a committee or commission authorized by subdivision 1 to request the issuance of subpoenas may, by a two-thirds vote of its members, request the issuance of an attachment to compel the attendance of a witness who, having been duly subpoenaed to attend, fails to do so. The chief clerk of the house of representatives or the secretary of the senate upon receipt of the request shall apply to the district court in Ramsey County for issuance of the attachment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.153.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Failure to respond.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who without lawful excuse fails to respond to a subpoena issued under this section or who, having been subpoenaed, willfully refuses to be sworn or affirm or to answer any material or proper question before a committee or commission is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.153.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/227/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 227 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/385/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 385 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.16">
        <h1 class="shn">3.16 MEMBERS, OFFICERS, AND ATTORNEYS EXCUSED FROM COURT DUTY.</h1>
        <p>No member or officer of, or attorney employed by, the legislature shall be compelled to attend as a witness in a court of this state during a session of the legislature, or while attending a meeting of a legislative committee or commission when the legislature is not in session unless the court in which the action is pending orders it, upon sufficient showing and with the consent of the presiding officer of the body of which the witness is an employee or the consent of the body of which the witness is a member. No cause or proceeding, civil or criminal, in court or before a commission or an officer or referee of a court or commission or a motion or hearing on the cause or proceeding, in which a member or officer of, or an attorney employed by, the legislature is a party, attorney, or witness shall be tried or heard during a session of the legislature or while the member, officer, or attorney is attending a meeting of a legislative committee or commission when the legislature is not in session. The matter shall be continued until the legislature or the committee or commission meeting has adjourned.</p>
        <p>The member, officer, or attorney may, with the consent of the body of the legislature of which the person is a member, officer, or employee, waive this privilege. The cause or proceeding, motion, or hearing may then be tried or heard at a time that will not conflict with legislative duties.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">40</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1909/0/51/#laws.0.1.0">1909 c 51 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1925/0/18/#laws.0.1.0">1925 c 18 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1927/0/47/#laws.0.1.0">1927 c 47 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1929/0/19/#laws.0.1.0">1929 c 19 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/45/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 45 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/183/#laws.0.1.0">1957 c 183 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.17">
        <h1 class="shn">3.17 JOURNALS.</h1>
        <p>A journal of the daily proceedings in each house shall be printed and laid before each member at the beginning of the next day's session. After it has been publicly read and corrected, a copy, kept by the secretary and chief clerk, respectively, and a transcript as approved shall be certified by the secretary or clerk to the printer, who shall print the corrected permanent journal. Executive messages, addresses, reports, communications, and voluminous documents other than amendments to the constitution or to bills and resolutions and the protests of members submitted under the constitution, article 4, section 11, shall be omitted from the journals, unless otherwise ordered by vote. Before distributing journals and other publications to members, legislative staff, and others, each house shall notify prospective recipients of the cost of the publications and the availability of the same information on the Internet.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.17.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">41</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.172.0">1976 c 2 s 172</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.36.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 36</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.18">
        <h1 class="shn">3.18 OTHER RECORDS.</h1>
        <p>Each house may determine, by rule or resolution, the number of copies of its journal to be printed, and the form and contents of its other records.</p>
        <p>It may have printed, in an appendix to its journal, the documents it desires. If both houses order the same document to be so printed, it shall be inserted only in the appendix to the senate journal.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">42</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.185">
        <h1 class="shn">3.185 ALTERING DRAFT OF BILL.</h1>
        <p>A person who fraudulently alters the draft of a bill or resolution which has been presented to either house of the legislature to be passed or adopted, with intent to procure its passage or adoption by either house or certification by the presiding officer in language different from that intended by the house, is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.185.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/96">10001</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/96">RL s 4816</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.19">
        <h1 class="shn">3.19 ENGROSSING AND ENROLLING.</h1>
        <p>All bills shall be engrossed or enrolled as provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3C.04">3C.04</a> and the rules of the senate and the house of representatives or their joint rules. In engrossing or enrolling bills, copying machines and other labor saving devices and equipment shall be used to the greatest possible extent.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">43</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1905/0/153/#laws.0.1.0">1905 c 153 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/366/#laws.0.1.0">1959 c 366 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/479/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 479 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.191">
        <h1 class="shn">3.191 ALTERING ENGROSSED BILL.</h1>
        <p>A person who fraudulently alters the engrossed copy or enrollment of a bill which has been passed by the legislature, with intent to procure its approval by the governor, certification by the secretary of state, or printing or publication by the printer of the statutes, in language different from that in which it was passed by the legislature, is guilty of a felony.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.191.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/96">10002</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/96">RL s 4817</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.192">
        <h1 class="shn">3.192 REQUIREMENTS FOR NEW OR RENEWED TAX EXPENDITURES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Any bill that creates, renews, or continues a tax expenditure must include a statement of intent that clearly provides the purpose of the tax expenditure and a standard or goal against which its effectiveness may be measured.</p>
        <p>(b) For purposes of this section, "tax expenditure" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.11#stat.270C.11.6">270C.11, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) Any bill that creates a new tax expenditure or continues an expiring tax expenditure must include an expiration date for the tax expenditure that is no more than eight years from the day the provision takes effect.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.192.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/389/#laws.10.1.0">2010 c 389 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.11.2.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 11 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.195">
        <h1 class="shn">3.195 REPORTS TO THE LEGISLATURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.195.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Distribution of reports.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in subdivision 4, a report to the legislature required of a department or agency shall be made, unless otherwise specifically required by law, by filing one copy with the Legislative Reference Library, and by making the report available electronically to the Legislative Reference Library. The same distribution procedure shall be followed for other reports and publications unless otherwise requested by a legislator or the Legislative Reference Library.</p>
          <p>(b) A public entity as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.073">16C.073</a> shall not distribute a report or publication to a member or employee of the legislature, except the Legislative Reference Library, unless the entity has determined that the member or employee wants the reports or publications published by that entity or the member or employee has requested the report or publication. This prohibition applies to both mandatory and voluntary reports and publications. A report or publication may be summarized in an executive summary and distributed as the entity chooses. Distribution of a report to legislative committee or commission members during a committee or commission hearing is not prohibited by this section.</p>
          <p>(c) A report or publication produced by a public entity may not be sent to both the home address and the office address of a representative or senator unless mailing to both addresses is requested by the representative or senator.</p>
          <p>(d) Reports, publications, periodicals, and summaries under this subdivision must be printed in a manner consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.073">16C.073</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.195.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Identification of documents.</span></h2>
          <p>When a report or publication as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.302#stat.3.302.3">3.302, subdivision 3</a>, is submitted by a department or agency to the Legislative Reference Library, the department or agency shall supply to the library the information necessary to identify the document as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.302#stat.3.302.3a">3.302, subdivision 3a</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.195.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Checklist of state documents.</span></h2>
          <p>The Legislative Reference Library shall monthly publish on its website a checklist of state documents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.195.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reports of criminal justice agencies; electronic versions only.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As used in this subdivision, "criminal justice agency" means the Departments of Corrections, Public Safety, and Human Rights; the Boards of Public Defense, Peace Officer Standards and Training, Private Detective and Protective Agent Services, and Judicial Standards; the Sentencing Guidelines and Uniform Laws Commissions; and the courts.</p>
          <p>(b) A criminal justice agency that submits a report to the legislature under this section shall do so by submitting an electronic version rather than a printed one. Notwithstanding subdivision 1, paragraph (a), and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.18">15.18</a>, the agency need submit only one electronic copy to the Legislative Reference Library, the State Library, and the Minnesota Historical Society. In addition, the agency shall submit one printed copy to the Legislative Reference Library.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.195.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/456/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 456 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/30/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 30 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/255/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 255 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 337 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/32/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 32 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/83/#laws.3.1.0">2009 c 83 art 3 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.5.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.10.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.196">
        <h1 class="shn">3.196 AUDITS.</h1>
        <p>The house of representatives and the senate shall each contract with the state auditor or a certified public accountant to perform an audit at least biennially.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.196.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.34.0">1993 c 192 s 34</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.197">
        <h1 class="shn">3.197 REQUIRED REPORTS.</h1>
        <p>A report to the legislature must contain, at the beginning of the report, the cost of preparing the report, including any costs incurred by another agency or another level of government.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.197.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/559/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 559 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.198"><b>3.198</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.42.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 42</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.1985">
        <h1 class="shn">3.1985 LEGISLATIVE FUNDING; APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.1985.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this section, "member expenses" means:</p>
          <p>(1) compensation to members of the legislature, to include salary; payroll taxes; leadership pay; employer-paid benefits or contributions offered through the state employee group insurance program or the Minnesota State Retirement System; and any fees related to items identified in this clause; and</p>
          <p>(2) per diem and mileage costs associated with the conduct of legislative business by members of the legislature, and housing and communication costs for members, as authorized by the house of representatives Committee on Rules and Legislative Administration or the senate Committee on Rules and Administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.1985.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Legislative funding.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Sums sufficient to fund member expenses of the house of representatives and the senate are appropriated from the general fund to the house of representatives and senate, as applicable.</p>
          <p>(b) No later than June 15 of each year, the controller of the house of representatives and the secretary of the senate must each certify to the commissioner of management and budget the amounts to be appropriated under this section for the fiscal year beginning July 1 of the same year.</p>
          <p>(c) No later than January 15 of each year, the controller of the house of representatives and the secretary of the senate must each certify to the commissioner of management and budget any changes to the current biennium's appropriations. Certifications provided by January 15 of an odd-numbered year must include estimated amounts to be appropriated for the fiscal biennium beginning the next July 1.</p>
          <p>(d) Amounts certified under paragraphs (b) and (c) must be the amounts determined by a majority vote conducted during a public meeting of the house of representatives Committee on Rules and Legislative Administration, or the senate Committee on Rules and Administration, as applicable.</p>
          <p>(e) At any time between the date funds are certified under this subdivision and the last date for adjusting the certified amount, the Legislative Advisory Commission may convene a meeting to review and provide advice on the certified amount. At its discretion, the committees may incorporate the advice of the Legislative Advisory Commission when making an adjustment to the certified amount.</p>
          <p>(f) Sums sufficient to address emergency needs of the house of representatives, senate, Legislative Coordinating Commission, and any other joint legislative office, council, or commission, are appropriated from the general fund to the house of representatives, senate, or Legislative Coordinating Commission, as applicable. Emergency needs may include but are not limited to information technology system failures, cybersecurity incidents, and physical infrastructure failures. The controller of the house of representatives, the secretary of the senate, or the executive director of the Legislative Coordinating Commission must certify to the commissioner of management and budget any amount to be appropriated under this paragraph, as directed by the speaker of the house, majority leader of the senate, or chair of the Legislative Coordinating Commission. To the extent practical, any amount proposed for appropriation must be submitted to the commissioner of management and budget for advice and comment prior to final certification. The total amount appropriated by this paragraph in a fiscal year must not exceed $1,000,000.</p>
          <p>(g) In the event of a nonappropriation caused by a gubernatorial veto impacting the house of representatives, the senate, the Legislative Coordinating Commission, or any other joint legislative office, council, or commission, the general fund appropriation base for the house of representatives, senate, or Legislative Coordinating Commission, plus three percent, is appropriated in the next fiscal year from the general fund to the house of representatives, senate, or Legislative Coordinating Commission, as applicable, for any expenses for which an appropriation is not otherwise provided by this section.</p>
          <p>(h) By October 15 each year, the house of representatives, the senate, and the Legislative Coordinating Commission must each submit a report to the commissioner of management and budget detailing expenditures made under paragraphs (a) and (f) for the prior fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.1985.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Other appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section precludes the house of representatives, the senate, or a joint legislative office or commission of the Legislative Coordinating Commission from receiving a direct appropriation by law or another statutory appropriation for a specific purpose provided in the direct or statutory appropriation. If the house of representatives, the senate, or a joint legislative office or commission receives a direct or statutory appropriation, the amount appropriated is distinct from and must not be considered during the biennial appropriation certification process under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.1985.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.11.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>This section, as added by Laws 2023, chapter 62, article 2, section 11, is effective July 1, 2025, and applies to appropriations for fiscal years 2026 and thereafter. Laws 2023, chapter 62, article 2, section 11, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.199">
        <h1 class="shn">3.199 ACCESSIBILITY IN THE LEGISLATURE'S INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.199.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following term has the meaning given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Responsible authority" means:</p>
          <p>(1) for the house of representatives, the chief clerk of the house;</p>
          <p>(2) for the senate, the secretary of the senate;</p>
          <p>(3) for the Office of the Revisor of Statutes, the revisor of statutes;</p>
          <p>(4) for the Office of the Legislative Auditor, the legislative auditor;</p>
          <p>(5) for the Legislative Reference Library, the library director;</p>
          <p>(6) for the Legislative Budget Office, the director of the Legislative Budget Office; and</p>
          <p>(7) for any entity administered by the legislative branch not listed in clauses (1) to (6), the director of the Legislative Coordinating Commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.199.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Accessibility standards; compliance.</span></h2>
          <p>The senate, the house of representatives, and joint legislative offices and commissions must comply with accessibility standards adopted for state agencies by the chief information officer under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.03">16E.03, subdivision </a>9, for technology, software, and hardware procurement, unless the responsible authority for a legislative body or office has approved an exception for a standard for that body or office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.199.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Not subject to the Department of Information Technology Services authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The chief information officer is not authorized to manage or direct compliance of the legislature with accessibility standards.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.199.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.5.1.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.2.16.0">2021 c 31 art 2 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AMENDMENTS TO CONSTITUTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.20">
        <h1 class="shn">3.20 FORM OF ACT; SUBMISSION.</h1>
        <p>Every act for the submission of an amendment to the constitution shall set forth the section as it will read if the amendment is adopted, with only the other matter necessary to show in what section or article the alteration is proposed. It shall be submitted and voted upon at the next general election as provided by the law relating to general elections. If adopted, the governor shall announce the fact by proclamation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.20.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">45</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.21">
        <h1 class="shn">3.21 NOTICE.</h1>
        <p>At least four months before the election, the attorney general shall furnish to the secretary of state a statement of the purpose and effect of all amendments proposed, showing clearly the form of the existing sections and how they will read if amended. If a section to which an amendment is proposed exceeds 150 words in length, the statement shall show the part of the section in which a change is proposed, both its existing form and as it will read when amended, together with the portions of the context that the attorney general deems necessary to understand the amendment.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">46</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/3">RL s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/152/#laws.0.0.0">1907 c 152</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1913/0/299/#laws.0.1.0">1913 c 299 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/136/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 136 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1951/0/699/#laws.0.1.0">1951 c 699 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/38/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 38 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/184/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 184 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/725/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 725 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/252/#laws.0.2.0">1979 c 252 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/543/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 543 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.60.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.3.17.0">1992 c 513 art 3 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.22"><b>3.22</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/201/#laws.0.82.0">2010 c 201 s 82</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY POLICY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.221">
        <h1 class="shn">3.221 COMMITTEES AND COMMISSIONS TO CONSIDER SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY POLICY.</h1>
        <p>Appropriate committees and commissions of the legislature must consider how proposed legislation that potentially affects scientific and technological development in the state conforms to the state's science and technology policy in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.222">3.222</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.221.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/467/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 467 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.222">
        <h1 class="shn">3.222 SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY POLICY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.222.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The science and technology policy in this section lists five goals that contribute to Minnesota's long-term economic growth. Development of these goals is critical if the state is to create an environment conducive to the growth and expansion of technology-based companies, as well as to improve the competitive ability of existing industries.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.222.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Encouragement and support of innovation and development of new technologies.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Minnesota has a long tradition of innovation and entrepreneurship. However, with the dramatic changes taking place in the global economy, the pace of technological change and shortened product life cycles, entrepreneurs and emerging technology-based companies are finding it increasingly difficult to compete effectively without appropriate resources. These entities represent the future of Minnesota's economy.</p>
          <p>(b) To give these entrepreneurs and emerging technology-based companies a greater chance at success, the state must support excellence in innovation and nurture their creative spirit by providing incentives to spur growth.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.222.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Support for industrial modernization and technology transfer to small companies.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The vast majority of Minnesota companies, both in rural and metropolitan areas, employ fewer than 50 employees. These small companies generally lack the resources to identify and implement available technologies that can help them modernize their industrial processes and develop their products in a more efficient manner. This is particularly pronounced in the manufacturing area.</p>
          <p>(b) The state must play a critical role in improving the competitive ability of these companies by making available information, technical expertise, and other services required to access existing, off-the-shelf technologies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.222.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Strengthen research and development partnerships between industry and academia.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Continued research and development is a prerequisite to the commercialization of new products and the growth of technology-based companies.</p>
          <p>(b) State government must play a significant role in supporting applied research and development initiatives. To maximize the impact, these initiatives in research and development must be closely tied to the research needs of the state's technology-based companies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.222.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Development of literate and technology skilled work force.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To compete in the future, communities will have to increasingly rely on knowledge-based economies. Not only will the work force of the future need to be more technically skilled than at present, but the basic level of literacy will also have to continually increase.</p>
          <p>(b) State government must continue to invest extensively in Minnesota's human capital and must produce more scientists and engineers. This investment is required throughout the educational system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.222.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Take advantage of opportunities in technology development.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Investment in programs that match federal funds for scientific and technological initiatives, match industry support, or otherwise support the development of research facilities is crucial to scientific and technological development in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(b) The state must have the ability to act on individual opportunities that may occur from time to time and that would enhance Minnesota's technology infrastructure.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.222.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
