      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.743"><b>3.743</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.744"><b>3.744</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.745"><b>3.745</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.746"><b>3.746</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.747"><b>3.747</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.748"><b>3.748</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.749">
        <h1 class="shn">3.749 LEGISLATIVE CLAIMS; FILING FEE.</h1>
        <p>A person filing a claim with the joint senate-house of representatives Subcommittee on Claims must pay a filing fee of $8. The money must be deposited by the clerk of the subcommittee in the state treasury and credited to the general fund. A claimant who is successful in obtaining an award from the subcommittee shall be reimbursed for the fee paid.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.749.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/620/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 620 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/148/#laws.3.4.0">2016 c 148 art 3 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.75"><b>3.75</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1066/#laws.0.19.0">1969 c 1066 s 19</a> subd 2]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.751">
        <h1 class="shn">3.751 CONTRACT CLAIMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.751.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Waiver of immunity.</span></h2>
          <p>When a controversy arises out of a contract for work, services, the delivery of goods, debt obligations of the state incurred under article XI of the Minnesota Constitution, or revenue obligations of a retirement fund incurred under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356B.10">356B.10</a> entered into by a state agency through established procedure, in respect to which controversy a party to the contract would be entitled to redress against the state in a court, if the state were suable, and no claim against the state has been made in a bill pending in the legislature for the same redress against it, the state waives immunity from suit in connection with the controversy and confers jurisdiction on the district court to determine it in the manner provided for civil actions in the district court. Only a party to the contract may bring action against the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.751.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Limitation of action.</span></h2>
          <p>No action shall be maintained unless it is commenced within 90 days after the plaintiff has been furnished by the state with a final estimate under the contract, or, at the election of the plaintiff, within six months after the work provided for under the contract is completed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.751.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Venue; procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>The action may be brought in the district court in the county where the cause of action or some part of it arose or in the district court in Ramsey County. The action shall be commenced by filing a complaint with the administrator of court and serving a summons and copy of the complaint upon the attorney general at the State Capitol. The state shall have 40 days from the date of the service to serve an answer upon the plaintiff. The action shall proceed in the district court as other actions at law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.751.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>An appeal from a final order or judgment in the action may be taken as in other civil cases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.751.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Trunk highways.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply to controversies arising out of a contract to construct or repair a state trunk highway.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.751.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/453/#laws.0.4.0">1961 c 453 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/331/#laws.0.34.0">1976 c 331 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/1/3/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp1982 c 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/247/#laws.0.3.0">1983 c 247 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/222/#laws.22.1.0">1999 c 222 art 22 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.52.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 52</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.752"><b>3.752</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/331/#laws.0.42.0">1976 c 331 s 42</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.753"><b>3.753</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/331/#laws.0.42.0">1976 c 331 s 42</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.754">
        <h1 class="shn">3.754 BUDGET REQUESTS; PROPERTY IMPROVEMENT CLAIMS.</h1>
        <p>All state departments and agencies including the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities shall include in their budget requests the amounts necessary to reimburse counties and municipalities for claims involving assessments for improvements benefiting state-owned property in their communities. Each department and agency shall pay the assessments when due or, if a department or agency feels that it was not fairly assessed, notify the chairs of the Committee on Finance of the senate and the Committee on Ways and Means of the house of representatives for a review of the assessment. Assessments on state-owned property under the control of the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/135A.131">135A.131</a>. All agencies and departments should negotiate assessment costs with counties and municipalities prior to commencement of improvements benefiting state-owned property.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.754.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/349/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 349 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/557/#laws.0.14.0">1974 c 557 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/321/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 321 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/620/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 620 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/395/#laws.0.18.0">1996 c 395 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/183/#laws.3.38.0">1997 c 183 art 3 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.755">
        <h1 class="shn">3.755 DAMAGE BY ESCAPING INMATES.</h1>
        <p>The Department of Corrections and Direct Care and Treatment shall pay all claims involving property damage, not covered by insurance, resulting from actions of escaping inmates or runaway patients occurring while making their escape. The department or agency must verify the reasonableness of the amounts claimed. Upon the approval of the Direct Care and Treatment executive board or the commissioner of corrections as to the institutions under their respective control, the superintendent or chief executive officer of an institution may pay out of the current expense appropriation of the institution to an employee of the institution the amount of any property damage sustained by the employee, not in excess of $500, because of action of a patient or inmate of the institution.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.755.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/557/#laws.0.12.0">1974 c 557 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/163/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 163 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/102/#laws.0.13.0">1979 c 102 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/128/#laws.0.2.0">2005 c 128 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.10.3.0">2024 c 79 art 10 s 3;</a> <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.42.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.42.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 42</a> </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.756">
        <h1 class="shn">3.756 MISDEMEANOR.</h1>
        <p>A person who knowingly and willfully presents, or attempts to present, a false or fraudulent claim; or a state officer who knowingly and willfully participates, or assists, in the preparation or presentation of a false or fraudulent claim is guilty of a misdemeanor. A state officer convicted of such an offense also forfeits office.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.756.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/899/#laws.0.19.0">1957 c 899 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.757">
        <h1 class="shn">3.757 RELEASE OF OPIOID-RELATED CLAIMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.757.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Municipality" has the meaning provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.01#stat.466.01.1">466.01, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) "Opioid litigation" means any civil litigation, demand, or settlement in lieu of litigation alleging unlawful conduct related to the marketing, sale, or distribution of opioids in this state or other alleged illegal actions that contributed to the excessive use of opioids.</p>
          <p>(d) "Released claim" means any cause of action or other claim that has been released in a statewide opioid settlement agreement, including matters identified as a released claim as that term or a comparable term is defined in a statewide opioid settlement agreement.</p>
          <p>(e) "Settling defendant" means Johnson &amp; Johnson, AmerisourceBergen Corporation, Cardinal Health, Inc., McKesson Corporation, Teva Pharmaceuticals, Allergan plc, CVS Health Corporation, Walgreens Boots Alliance, Inc., and Walmart, Inc., as well as related subsidiaries, affiliates, officers, directors, and other related entities specifically named as a released entity in a statewide opioid settlement agreement.</p>
          <p>(f) "Statewide opioid settlement agreement" means an agreement, including consent judgments, assurances of discontinuance, and related agreements or documents, between the attorney general, on behalf of the state, and a settling defendant, to provide or allocate remuneration for conduct related to the marketing, sale, or distribution of opioids in this state or other alleged illegal actions that contributed to the excessive use of opioids.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.757.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Release of claims.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No municipality shall have the authority to assert, file, or enforce a released claim against a settling defendant.</p>
          <p>(b) Any claim in pending opioid litigation filed by a municipality against a settling defendant that is within the scope of a released claim is extinguished by operation of law.</p>
          <p>(c) The attorney general shall have authority to appear or intervene in opioid litigation where a municipality has asserted, filed, or enforced a released claim against a settling defendant and release with prejudice any released claims.</p>
          <p>(d) This section does not limit any causes of action, claims, or remedies, nor the authority to assert, file, or enforce such causes of action, claims, or remedies, by a party other than a municipality.</p>
          <p>(e) This section does not limit any causes of action, claims, or remedies, nor the authority to assert, file, or enforce such causes of action, claims, or remedies by a municipality against entities and individuals other than a released claim against a settling defendant.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.757.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/53/#laws.0.1.0">2022 c 53 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/50/#laws.4.1.0">2023 c 50 art 4 s 1</a></p>
      </div>

</div>

      <h1 class="fgh">ADMINISTRATIVE RULES REVIEW</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.841">
        <h1 class="shn">3.841 LEGISLATIVE COORDINATING COMMISSION.</h1>
        <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.842">3.842</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.843">3.843</a>, "commission" means the Legislative Coordinating Commission or a legislative commission or subcommittee established by the coordinating commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.305#stat.3.305.6">3.305, subdivision 6</a>, to exercise the powers and discharge the duties of the coordinating commission under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.842">3.842</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.843">3.843</a> or other law requiring action by the coordinating commission on matters relating to administrative rules.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.841.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/355/#laws.0.69.0">1974 c 355 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/615/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 615 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/618/#laws.0.26.0">1980 c 618 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/112/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 112 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/253/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 253 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.2.1.0">1981 c 342 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/155/#laws.0.6.0">1989 c 155 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/370/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 370 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/629/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 629 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 98 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.842">
        <h1 class="shn">3.842 REVIEW OF RULES BY COMMISSION OR COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall promote adequate and proper rules by agencies and an understanding upon the part of the public respecting them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Jurisdiction.</span></h2>
          <p>The jurisdiction of the commission includes all rules as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.02#stat.14.02.4">14.02, subdivision 4</a>. The commission also has jurisdiction of rules filed with the secretary of state in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.38">14.38</a>, subdivision 11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.388">14.388</a>.</p>
          <p>The commission may periodically review statutory exemptions to the rulemaking provisions of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Hearings.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission may hold public hearings to investigate complaints with respect to rules if it considers the complaints meritorious and worthy of attention. If the rules that are the subject of the public hearing were adopted without a rulemaking hearing, it may request the Office of Administrative Hearings to hold the public hearing and prepare a report summarizing the testimony received at the hearing. The Office of Administrative Hearings shall assess the costs of the public hearing to the agency whose rules are the subject of the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 98 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Objections to rules.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "committee" means the house of representatives policy committee or senate policy committee with primary jurisdiction over state governmental operations. The commission or a committee may object to a rule as provided in this subdivision. If the commission or a committee objects to all or some portion of a rule because the commission or committee considers it to be beyond the procedural or substantive authority delegated to the agency, including a proposed rule submitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.15#stat.14.15.4">14.15, subdivision 4</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.26#stat.14.26.3">14.26, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c), the commission or committee may file that objection in the Office of the Secretary of State. The filed objection must contain a concise statement of the commission's or committee's reasons for its action. An objection to a proposed rule submitted by the commission or a committee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.15#stat.14.15.4">14.15, subdivision 4</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.26#stat.14.26.3">14.26, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c), may not be filed before the rule is adopted.</p>
          <p>(b) The secretary of state shall affix to each objection a certification of the date and time of its filing and as soon after the objection is filed as practicable shall transmit a certified copy of it to the agency issuing the rule in question and to the revisor of statutes. The secretary of state shall also maintain a permanent register open to public inspection of all objections by the commission or committee.</p>
          <p>(c) The commission or committee shall publish and index an objection filed under this section in the next issue of the State Register. The revisor of statutes shall indicate the existence of the objection adjacent to the rule in question when that rule is published in Minnesota Rules.</p>
          <p>(d) Within 14 days after the filing of an objection by the commission or committee to a rule, the issuing agency shall respond in writing to the objecting entity. After receipt of the response, the commission or committee may withdraw or modify its objection.</p>
          <p>(e) After the filing of an objection by the commission or committee that is not subsequently withdrawn, the burden is upon the agency in any proceeding for judicial review or for enforcement of the rule to establish that the whole or portion of the rule objected to is valid.</p>
          <p>(f) The failure of the commission or a committee to object to a rule is not an implied legislative authorization of its validity.</p>
          <p>(g) In accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.44">14.44</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.45">14.45</a>, the commission or a committee may petition for a declaratory judgment to determine the validity of a rule objected to by the commission or committee. The action must be started within two years after an objection is filed in the Office of the Secretary of State.</p>
          <p>(h) The commission or a committee may intervene in litigation arising from agency action. For purposes of this paragraph, agency action means the whole or part of a rule, or the failure to issue a rule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 98 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 98 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.842.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 98 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.842.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/355/#laws.0.69.0">1974 c 355 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/615/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 615 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/618/#laws.0.26.0">1980 c 618 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/112/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 112 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/253/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 253 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.2.1.0">1981 c 342 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.1.4.0">1984 c 655 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.84.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/155/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 155 s 2</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/629/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 629 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.1.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.2.0">1997 c 98 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/469/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 469 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.1.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.843">
        <h1 class="shn">3.843 PUBLIC HEARINGS BY STATE AGENCIES.</h1>
        <p>By a vote of a majority of its members, the commission may request any agency issuing rules to hold a public hearing in respect to recommendations made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.842">3.842</a>, including recommendations made by the commission to promote adequate and proper rules by that agency and recommendations contained in the commission's biennial report. The agency shall give notice as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.14#stat.14.14.1">14.14, subdivision 1</a>, of a hearing under this section, to be conducted in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.05">14.05</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.28">14.28</a>. The hearing must be held not more than 60 days after receipt of the request or within any other longer time period specified by the commission in the request.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.843.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/355/#laws.0.69.0">1974 c 355 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/615/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 615 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/618/#laws.0.26.0">1980 c 618 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/112/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 112 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/253/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 253 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/342/#laws.2.1.0">1981 c 342 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/155/#laws.0.6.0">1989 c 155 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.56.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.4.0">1997 c 98 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.844"><b>3.844</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 98 s 17</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.845"><b>3.845</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/98/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 98 s 17</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.846"><b>3.846</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.57.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 57</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON PENSIONS AND RETIREMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.85">
        <h1 class="shn">3.85 LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON PENSIONS AND RETIREMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>The Legislative Commission on Pensions and Retirement is created to study and investigate public retirement systems.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Powers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall make a continuing study and investigation of retirement benefit plans applicable to nonfederal government employees in this state. The powers and duties of the commission include, but are not limited to the following:</p>
          <p>(a) studying retirement benefit plans applicable to nonfederal government employees in Minnesota, including federal plans available to the employees;</p>
          <p>(b) making recommendations within the scope of its study, including attention to financing of the various pension funds and financing of accrued liabilities;</p>
          <p>(c) considering all aspects of pension planning and operation and making recommendations designed to establish and maintain sound pension policy for all funds;</p>
          <p>(d) analyzing each item of proposed pension and retirement legislation, including amendments to each, with particular reference to analysis of their cost, actuarial soundness, and adherence to sound pension policy, and reporting its findings to the legislature;</p>
          <p>(e) creating and maintaining a library for reference concerning pension and retirement matters, including information about laws and systems in other states; and</p>
          <p>(f) studying, analyzing, and preparing reports in regard to subjects certified to the commission for study.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission consists of seven members of the senate appointed by the Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration and seven members of the house of representatives appointed by the speaker. No more than five members from each chamber may be from the majority caucus in that chamber. Members shall be appointed at the commencement of each regular session of the legislature for a two-year term beginning January 16 of the first year of the regular session. Members continue to serve until their successors are appointed. Vacancies that occur while the legislature is in session shall be filled like regular appointments. If the legislature is not in session, senate vacancies shall be filled by the last Subcommittee on Committees of the senate Committee on Rules and Administration or other appointing authority designated by the senate rules, and house of representatives vacancies shall be filled by the last speaker of the house, or if the speaker is not available, by the last chair of the house of representatives Rules Committee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Office, meetings, officers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall maintain an office in the Capitol group of buildings in space which the commissioner of administration shall provide. The commission shall hold meetings at the times and places it may designate. It shall select a chair, a vice-chair, and other officers from its membership as it deems necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission may employ professional and technical assistants as it deems necessary to perform the duties prescribed in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Assistance of other agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission may request information from any state officer or agency or public pension fund or plan as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.63">356.63, paragraph (b)</a>, including a firefighters relief association to which sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/424A.091">424A.091</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/424A.096">424A.096</a> apply, to assist it to carry out the terms of this section. The officer, agency, or public pension fund or plan shall promptly furnish any data requested.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Expenses, reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>The members of the commission and its staff shall be reimbursed for all expenses actually and necessarily incurred in the performance of their duties. Reimbursement for expenses incurred shall be made in accordance with policies adopted by the Legislative Coordinating Commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Expenses and reports.</span></h2>
          <p>Expenses of the commission shall be approved by the chair or another member as the rules of the commission provide.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Standards for pension valuations and cost estimates.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commission shall adopt standards prescribing specific detailed methods to calculate, evaluate, and display current and proposed law liabilities, costs, and actuarial equivalents of all public employee pension plans in Minnesota. These standards shall be consistent with chapter 356 and be updated annually. At a minimum, the standards shall contain requirements that comply with generally accepted accounting principles applicable to government pension plans. The standards may include additional financial, funding, or valuation requirements that are not required under generally accepted accounting principles applicable to government pension plans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/223/#laws.0.11.0">2004 c 223 s 11</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.85.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/223/#laws.0.11.0">2004 c 223 s 11</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.85.history
			">

      <h1 class="fgh">EMPLOYEE RELATIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.855">
        <h1 class="shn">3.855 EMPLOYEE RELATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.855.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 248 art 2 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.855.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commission" means the Legislative Coordinating Commission or a legislative commission established by the coordinating commission, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.305#stat.3.305.6">3.305, subdivision 6</a>, to exercise the powers and discharge the duties of the coordinating commission under this section or other law requiring action by the coordinating commission on matters of public employment or compensation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.855.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote"> State employee compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of management and budget shall submit to the chair of the commission any compensation plans or salaries prepared under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.18">43A.18, subdivisions 2</a>, 3, 3b, and 4. The chancellor of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities shall submit any compensation plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.18#stat.43A.18.3a">43A.18, subdivision 3a</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The proposed salary or compensation plan must be implemented upon its submission to the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.855.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Other salary and compensation plan.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commission shall review and approve or reject the plan for compensation, terms, and conditions of employment of classified employees in the office of the legislative auditor under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.971#stat.3.971.2">3.971, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.855.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Other duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall:</p>
          <p>(1) continually monitor the state's civil service system provided for in chapter 43A, rules of the commissioner of management and budget, and the collective bargaining process provided for in chapter 179A, as applied to state employees;</p>
          <p>(2) research and analyze the need for improvements in those statutory sections;</p>
          <p>(3) adopt rules consistent with this section relating to the scheduling and conduct of commission business and other organizational and procedural matters; and</p>
          <p>(4) perform other related functions delegated to it by the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.855.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>MS 2023 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.72.8.0">2024 c 127 art 72 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.855.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Information required; collective bargaining agreements, memoranda of understanding, and interest arbitration awards.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 14 days after the implementation of a collective bargaining agreement, memorandum of understanding, compensation plan, or receipt of an interest arbitration award, the commissioner of management and budget must submit to the Legislative Coordinating Commission the following:</p>
          <p>(1) a copy of the collective bargaining agreement or compensation plan showing changes from previous agreements and a copy of the executed agreement;</p>
          <p>(2) a copy of any memorandum of understanding that has a fiscal impact or interest arbitration award;</p>
          <p>(3) a comparison of biennial compensation costs under the current agreement or plan to the projected biennial compensation costs under the new agreement, memorandum of understanding, or interest arbitration award; and</p>
          <p>(4) a comparison of biennial compensation costs under the current agreement or plan to the projected biennial compensation costs for the following biennium under the new agreement, memorandum of understanding, or interest arbitration award.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.855.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/332/#laws.1.2.0">1979 c 332 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/617/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 617 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/314/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 314 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/560/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 560 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/299/#laws.0.2.0">1983 c 299 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/462/#laws.0.27.0">1984 c 462 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>;       </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.865"><b>3.865</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.8.20.0">1991 c 265 art 8 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.866"><b>3.866</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.8.20.0">1991 c 265 art 8 s 20</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.873"><b>3.873</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.1.83.0">1999 c 86 art 1 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.875"><b>3.875</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.66.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 66</a> subd 8]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.882"><b>3.882</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 248 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.884"><b>3.884</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/133/#laws.2.13.0">2007 c 133 art 2 s 13</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON METROPOLITAN GOVERNMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.8841">
        <h1 class="shn">3.8841 LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON METROPOLITAN GOVERNMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Established.</span></h2>
          <p>The Legislative Commission on Metropolitan Government is established to oversee the Metropolitan Council's operating and capital budgets, work program, and capital improvement program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission consists of four senators appointed by the senate Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration, three senators appointed by the senate minority leader, four state representatives appointed by the speaker of the house, and three state representatives appointed by the house minority leader. All members must reside in or represent a portion of the seven-county metropolitan area. The appointing authorities must ensure balanced geographic representation. Each appointing authority must make appointments as soon as possible after the opening of the next regular session of the legislature in each odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Terms; vacancies.</span></h2>
          <p>Members of the commission serve for a two-year term beginning upon appointment and expiring upon appointment of a successor after the opening of the next regular session of the legislature in the odd-numbered year. A vacancy in the membership of the commission must be filled for the unexpired term in a manner that will preserve the representation established by this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Chair.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must meet as soon as practicable after members are appointed in each odd-numbered year to elect its chair and other officers as it may determine necessary. A chair serves a two-year term, expiring in the odd-numbered year after a successor is elected. The chair must alternate biennially between the senate and the house of representatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>Members serve without compensation but may be reimbursed for their reasonable expenses as members of the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>Legislative staff must provide administrative and research assistance to the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Meetings; procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission meets at the call of the chair. If there is a quorum, the commission may take action by a simple majority vote of commission members present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Powers; duties; Metropolitan Council levy, budget oversight.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must monitor, review, and make recommendations to the Metropolitan Council and to the legislature for the following calendar year on:</p>
          <p>(1) the tax rate and dollar amount of the Metropolitan Council's property tax levies and any proposed increases in the rate or dollar amount of tax;</p>
          <p>(2) any request for an increase in the debt of the Metropolitan Council;</p>
          <p>(3) the overall work and role of the Metropolitan Council;</p>
          <p>(4) the Metropolitan Council's proposed operating and capital budgets, work program, and capital improvement program; and</p>
          <p>(5) the Metropolitan Council's implementation of the operating and capital budgets, work program, and capital improvement program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8841.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Powers; duties; Metropolitan Council appointments oversight.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must monitor appointments to the Metropolitan Council and may make recommendations on appointments to the nominating committee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.123#stat.473.123.3">473.123, subdivision 3</a>, or to the governor before the governor makes the appointments. The commission may also make recommendations to the senate before appointments are presented to the senate for its advice and consent.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.8841.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.13.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON<br>MINNESOTA SPORTS FACILITIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.8842">
        <h1 class="shn">3.8842 LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON MINNESOTA SPORTS FACILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The Legislative Commission on Minnesota Sports Facilities is established by and under the authority of the Legislative Coordinating Commission to oversee the Minnesota Sports Facilities Authority's operating and capital budgets. The legislature finds that continuous legislative review of the financial management of the authority is necessary to promote fiscal responsibility and good management, and strengthen the accountability of the authority. The commission is charged with:</p>
          <p>(1) providing financial oversight of the authority as described in subdivision 8;</p>
          <p>(2) adoption of a statewide authority structure for the operation and management of sports facilities and entertainment venues under the jurisdiction of the authority. The authority membership shall represent the interests of both the metropolitan area and greater Minnesota; and</p>
          <p>(3) creating a comprehensive management plan that alleviates booking and scheduling concerns regarding the sports facilities and entertainment venues under the jurisdiction of the authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission consists of three senators appointed by the senate majority leader, three senators appointed by the senate minority leader, three state representatives appointed by the speaker of the house, and three state representatives appointed by the house minority leader. The appointing authorities must ensure balanced geographic representation. Each appointing authority must make appointments as soon as possible after the opening of the next regular session of the legislature in each odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Terms; vacancies.</span></h2>
          <p>Members of the commission serve for a two-year term beginning upon appointment and expiring upon appointment of a successor after the opening of the next regular session of the legislature in the odd-numbered year. A vacancy in the membership of the commission must be filled for the unexpired term in a manner that will preserve the representation established by this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Chair.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must meet as soon as practicable after members are appointed in each odd-numbered year to elect its chair and other officers as it may determine necessary. A chair serves a two-year term, expiring in the odd-numbered year after a successor is elected. The chair must alternate biennially between the senate and the house of representatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>Members serve without compensation but may be reimbursed for their reasonable expenses as members of the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>Legislative staff must provide administrative and research assistance to the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Meetings; procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission meets at least semiannually. If there is a quorum, the commission may take action by a simple majority vote of commission members present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Powers; duties; Minnesota Sports Facilities Authority, budget oversight.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must monitor, review, and make recommendations to the authority and to the legislature for the following calendar year on:</p>
          <p>(1) any proposed increases in the rate or dollar amount of tax;</p>
          <p>(2) any proposed increases in the debt of the authority;</p>
          <p>(3) the overall work and role of the authority;</p>
          <p>(4) the authority's proposed operating and capital budgets;</p>
          <p>(5) the authority's implementation of the operating and capital budgets; and</p>
          <p>(6) any other topics as deemed necessary by the commission to fulfill the purpose described in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8842.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall report on January 15 of the even-numbered year on the effectiveness and future prospects of the commission.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.8842.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/299/#laws.1.1.0">2012 c 299 art 1 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.8843"><b>3.8843</b> MS 2018 [Expired, 1Sp2017 c 4 art 2 s 2]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON DATA PRACTICES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.8844">
        <h1 class="shn">3.8844 LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON DATA PRACTICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8844.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Established.</span></h2>
          <p>The Legislative Commission on Data Practices and Personal Data Privacy is created to study issues relating to government data practices and individuals' personal data privacy rights and to review legislation impacting data practices, data security, and personal data privacy. The commission is a continuation of the commission that was established by Laws 2014, chapter 193, as amended, and which expired June 30, 2019.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8844.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission consists of two senators appointed by the senate majority leader, two senators appointed by the minority leader in the senate, two members of the house of representatives appointed by the speaker, and two members of the house of representatives appointed by the minority leader in the house. Two members from each chamber must be from the majority party in that chamber and two members from each chamber must be from the minority party in that chamber. Each appointing authority must make appointments as soon as possible after the beginning of the regular legislative session in the odd-numbered year. The ranking senator from the majority party appointed to the commission must convene the first meeting of a biennium by February 15 in the odd-numbered year. The commission may elect up to four former legislators who have demonstrated an interest in, or have a history of working in, the areas of government data practices and personal data privacy to serve as nonvoting members of the commission. The former legislators must not be registered lobbyists and shall be compensated as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575#stat.15.0575.3">15.0575, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8844.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Terms; vacancies.</span></h2>
          <p>Members of the commission serve for terms beginning upon appointment and ending at the beginning of the regular legislative session in the next odd-numbered year. The appropriate appointing authority must fill a vacancy for a seat of a current legislator for the remainder of the unexpired term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8844.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Officers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must elect a chair and may elect other officers as it determines are necessary. The chair alternates between a member of the senate and a member of the house of representatives in January of each odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8844.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>Legislative staff must provide administrative and research assistance to the commission. The Legislative Coordinating Commission may, if funding is available, appoint staff to provide research assistance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8844.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall:</p>
          <p>(1) review and provide the legislature with research and analysis of emerging issues relating to government data practices and security and privacy of personal data;</p>
          <p>(2) review and make recommendations on legislative proposals relating to the Minnesota Government Data Practices Act; and</p>
          <p>(3) review and make recommendations on legislative proposals impacting personal data privacy rights, data security, and other related issues.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.8844.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 3 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON HOUSING AFFORDABILITY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.8845">
        <h1 class="shn">3.8845 LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON HOUSING AFFORDABILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8845.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Legislative Commission on Housing Affordability consists of:</p>
          <p>(1) two senators appointed by the senate majority leader;</p>
          <p>(2) two senators appointed by the senate minority leader;</p>
          <p>(3) two representatives appointed by the speaker of the house; and</p>
          <p>(4) two representatives appointed by the minority leader of the house of representatives.</p>
          <p>(b) Each appointing authority must make appointments by January 31 of the regular legislative session in the odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8845.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>The ranking senator from the majority party appointed to the commission must convene the first meeting of a biennium by February 15 in the odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8845.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Terms; vacancies.</span></h2>
          <p>Members of the commission serve for terms beginning upon appointment and ending at the beginning of the regular legislative session in the next odd-numbered year. The appropriate appointing authority must fill a vacancy for a seat of a current legislator for the remainder of the unexpired term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8845.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Officers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must elect a chair and may elect other officers as it determines are necessary at the first meeting of the commission in an odd-numbered year. The chair alternates between a member of the senate and a member of the house of representatives at the start of the regular legislative session in each odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8845.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>The Legislative Coordinating Commission must provide administrative and research assistance to the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8845.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall:</p>
          <p>(1) define housing affordability and study issues relating to housing affordability and the construction, preservation, and rehabilitation of owner-occupied and rental housing, including subsidized housing, existing and future government regulations impacting housing affordability, market forces impacting housing affordability, and access to homeownership;</p>
          <p>(2) review and provide the legislature with research and analysis of emerging issues affecting housing affordability and homeownership access, including but not limited to construction work force, innovation, building practices, and building material costs;</p>
          <p>(3) review and provide the legislature with research and analysis of policies to reduce the homeownership equity gap; and</p>
          <p>(4) review and make recommendations on legislative and rulemaking proposals positively impacting personal housing affordability, access to homeownership, and other related barriers to homeownership, especially with regard to first-time home buyers and economically disadvantaged buyers and renters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8845.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>This section expires June 30, 2023.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.8845.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 2 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON<br>PLANNING AND FISCAL POLICY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.885">
        <h1 class="shn">3.885 LEGISLATIVE COMMISSION ON PLANNING AND FISCAL POLICY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The Legislative Commission on Planning and Fiscal Policy consists of nine members of the senate appointed by the Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration and nine members of the house of representatives appointed by the speaker. Vacancies on the commission are filled in the same manner as original appointments. The commission shall elect a chair and a vice-chair from among its members. The chair alternates between a member of the senate and a member of the house of representatives in January of each odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 248 art 2 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>Members of the commission are compensated as provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.101">3.101</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 248 art 2 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agencies to cooperate.</span></h2>
          <p>All departments, agencies, and education institutions of the executive and judicial branches must comply with a request of the commission for information, data, estimates, and statistics on the funding revenue operations, and other affairs of the department, agency, or education institution. The commissioner of management and budget and the commissioner of revenue shall provide the commission with full and free access to information, data, estimates, and statistics in the possession of the Management and Budget and Revenue Departments on the state budget, revenue, expenditures, and tax expenditures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commission shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide the legislature with research and analysis of current and projected state revenue, state expenditures, and state tax expenditures;</p>
          <p>(2) provide the legislature with a report analyzing the governor's proposed levels of revenue and expenditures for biennial budgets submitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.11">16A.11</a> as well as other supplemental budget submittals to the legislature by the governor;</p>
          <p>(3) provide an analysis of the impact of the governor's proposed revenue and expenditure plans for the next biennium;</p>
          <p>(4) conduct research on matters of economic and fiscal policy and report to the legislature on the result of the research;</p>
          <p>(5) provide economic reports and studies on the state of the state's economy, including trends and forecasts for consideration by the legislature;</p>
          <p>(6) conduct budget and tax studies and provide general fiscal and budgetary information;</p>
          <p>(7) review and make recommendations on the operation of state programs in order to appraise the implementation of state laws regarding the expenditure of funds and to recommend means of improving their efficiency;</p>
          <p>(8) recommend to the legislature changes in the mix of revenue sources for programs, in the percentage of state expenditures devoted to major programs, and in the role of the legislature in overseeing state government expenditures and revenue projections;</p>
          <p>(9) make a continuing study and investigation of the building needs of the government of the state of Minnesota, including, but not limited to the following: the current and future requirements of new buildings, the maintenance of existing buildings, rehabilitating and remodeling of old buildings, the planning for administrative offices, and the exploring of methods of financing building and related costs; and</p>
          <p>(10) conduct a continuing study of state-local finance, analyzing and making recommendations to the legislature on issues including levels of state support for political subdivisions, basic levels of local need, balances of local revenues and options, relationship of local taxes to individuals' ability to pay, and financial reporting by political subdivisions. In conducting this study, the commission shall consult with the governor, the staff of executive branch agencies, and the governor's Advisory Commission on State-Local Relations.</p>
          <p>(b) In performing its duties under paragraph (a), the commission shall consider, among other things:</p>
          <p>(1) the relative dependence on state tax revenues, federal funds, and user fees to support state-funded programs, and whether the existing mix of revenue sources is appropriate, given the purposes of the programs;</p>
          <p>(2) the relative percentages of state expenditures that are devoted to major programs such as education, assistance to local government, aid to individuals, state agencies and institutions, and debt service; and</p>
          <p>(3) the role of the legislature in overseeing state government expenditures, including legislative appropriation of money from the general fund, legislative appropriation of money from funds other than the general fund, state agency receipt of money into revolving and other dedicated funds and expenditure of money from these funds, and state agency expenditure of federal funds.</p>
          <p>(c) The commission's recommendations must consider the long-term needs of the state. The recommendations must not duplicate work done by standing committees of the senate and house of representatives.</p>
          <p>The commission shall report to the legislature on its activities and recommendations by January 15 of each odd-numbered year.</p>
          <p>The commission shall provide the public with printed and electronic copies of reports and information for the legislature. Copies must be provided at the actual cost of furnishing each copy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 248 art 2 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 248 art 2 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 248 art 2 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.3.37.0">2013 c 142 art 3 s 37</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.885.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Review of advisory groups.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By September 1 of each odd-numbered year, the commission shall compile a list of executive branch advisory groups created in statute. The commission may develop a schedule for review of advisory groups, or may select particular groups for review. By December 31 of each odd-numbered year, the commission may make recommendations on the continuing need for certain advisory groups, and on any changes in laws governing a group that are needed to improve the group's efficiency and effectiveness.</p>
          <p>(b) In conducting reviews of executive branch advisory groups, the commission shall consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the mission of each group, and the extent to which the mission has been satisfied;</p>
          <p>(2) the extent to which each advisory group is effective in allowing persons interested in the program or activity for which the group provides advice to have input into the operations of the state agency implementing the program or activity;</p>
          <p>(3) the extent to which the existence of the advisory group provides state agencies with an efficient and effective means of obtaining expert advice and opinions;</p>
          <p>(4) whether there are more efficient and effective methods of accomplishing the mission of the advisory group; and</p>
          <p>(5) whether the work of the advisory group overlaps or duplicates the work of other groups.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.885.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.63.0">1987 c 404 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/686/#laws.1.32.0">1988 c 686 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/155/#laws.0.6.0">1989 c 155 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.1.4.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 1 s 4</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.1.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.2.5.0">1991 c 345 art 2 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.42.0">2008 c 204 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/318/#laws.1.1.0">2008 c 318 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.2.1.0">2013 c 142 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEGISLATIVE ENERGY COMMISSION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.8851">
        <h1 class="shn">3.8851 LEGISLATIVE ENERGY COMMISSION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8851.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) There is established a Legislative Energy Commission to study and to make recommendations for legislation concerning issues related to its duties under subdivision 3.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commission consists of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) nine members of the house of representatives, five of whom are appointed by the speaker of the house and four of whom are appointed by the leader of the minority caucus; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) nine members of the senate, five of whom are appointed by the leader of the majority caucus and four of whom are appointed by the leader of the minority caucus.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commission may employ full-time and part-time staff, contract for consulting services, and may reimburse the expenses of persons requested to assist it in its duties. The director of the Legislative Coordinating Commission shall assist the commission in administrative matters. The commission shall elect cochairs, one member of the house of representatives and one member of the senate from among the committee and subcommittee chairs named to the commission. The commission members from the house of representatives shall elect the house of representatives cochair, and the commission members from the senate shall elect the senate cochair.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8851.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Subcommittees.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commission may establish subcommittees as necessary to perform its duties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8851.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commission shall continuously evaluate the energy policies of this state and the degree to which they promote an environmentally and economically sustainable energy future. The commission shall monitor the state's progress in achieving its goals to develop renewable sources of electric energy under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.1691#stat.216B.1691.2a">216B.1691, subdivision 2a</a>, and the progress of energy-related sectors in reducing greenhouse gas emissions under the state's greenhouse gas emissions-reductions goals established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216H.02#stat.216H.02.1">216H.02, subdivision 1</a>. The commission may review proposed energy legislation and may recommend legislation. The commission shall when feasible solicit and consider public testimony regarding the economic, environmental, and social implications of state energy plans and policies. Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary the commission's evaluations and reviews under this subdivision shall include new and existing technologies for nuclear power.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commission may study, analyze, hold hearings, and make legislative recommendations regarding the following issues:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the generation, transmission, and distribution of electricity;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the conservation of energy;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) alternative energy sources available to replace dwindling fossil fuel and other nonrenewable fuel sources;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the development of renewable energy supplies;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) the economic development potential associated with issues described in clauses (1) to (5); and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) other energy-related subjects the commission finds significant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8851.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nuclear reports.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The public utility that owns the Prairie Island and Monticello nuclear generation facilities shall update the reports required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116C.772#stat.116C.772.3">116C.772</a>, subdivisions 3 to 5, and shall submit those updates periodically to the Public Utilities Commission with the utility's resource plan filing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2422">216B.2422</a> and to the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8851.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Subpoena power.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commission may issue a subpoena under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.153">3.153</a> to any person for production of information held by that person that is relevant to the work of the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8851.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Data from state agencies.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A state agency shall reply promptly to a request for data from the commission, subject to the requirements of chapter 13 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.17">15.17</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.8851.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Assessment; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Upon request by the cochairs of the commission, the commissioner of commerce shall assess the amount requested for the operation of the commission, not to exceed $250,000 in a fiscal year, from the following sources:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) 50 percent of the assessment must come from all public utilities, municipal utilities, electric cooperative associations, generation and transmission cooperative electric associations, and municipal power agencies providing electric or natural gas services in Minnesota; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) 50 percent of the assessment must come from all bulk terminals located in this state from which petroleum products and liquid petroleum gas are dispensed.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner of commerce shall apportion the assessment amount requested among the entities in paragraph (a), clause (1), in proportion to their respective gross operating revenues from energy sold within the state during the most recent calendar year.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner of commerce shall apportion the assessment amount requested equally among the referenced entities in paragraph (a), clause (2).</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The entities in paragraph (a), clause (1), must provide information to the commissioner of commerce to allow for calculation of the assessment.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The assessments under this subdivision are in addition to assessments made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.62">216B.62</a>. The amount assessed under this section must be deposited in the Legislative Energy Commission account in the special revenue fund. Funds in the Legislative Energy Commission account are appropriated to the director of the Legislative Coordinating Commission for the purposes of this section, and are available until expended. Utilities selling gas and electric service at retail must be assessed and billed in accordance with the procedures provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.62">216B.62</a>, to the extent that these procedures do not conflict with this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.8851.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
