<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.3.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 3</a>,4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.972">
        <h1 class="shn">3.972 AUDITS OF AGENCIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Public accountant.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "public accountant" means a certified public accountant or certified public accounting firm licensed in accordance with chapter 326A.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Audits of state and semistate agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislative auditor shall, as resources permit, audit the financial activities of: (1) all departments, agencies, offices, and other organizations in the state executive branch; (2) courts, offices, and other organizations in the state judicial branch; and (3) public boards, associations, societies, and other public organizations created by state law or supported, wholly or in part, by state funds. The legislative auditor shall ascertain that all financial transactions and operations involving the public funds and property of the state comply with the spirit and purpose of the law, are sound by modern standards of financial management, and are for the best protection of public funds and other public resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Audits of Department of Human Services.</span></h2>
          <p> To ensure effective legislative oversight and accountability, the legislative auditor shall give high priority to auditing the programs, services, and benefits administered by the Department of Human Services. As resources permit, the legislative auditor shall track and assess expenditures throughout the human service delivery system, from the department to the point of service delivery, and determine whether human services programs, services, and benefits are being provided cost-effectively and only to eligible individuals and organizations in compliance with applicable legal requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Audits of managed care organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The legislative auditor shall audit each managed care organization that contracts with the commissioner of human services to provide health care services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.69">256B.69</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.692">256B.692</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256L.12">256L.12</a>. The legislative auditor shall design the audits to determine if a managed care organization used the public money in compliance with federal and state laws, rules, and in accordance with provisions in the managed care organization's contract with the commissioner of human services. The legislative auditor shall determine the schedule and scope of the audit work and may contract with vendors to assist with the audits. The managed care organization must cooperate with the legislative auditor and must provide the legislative auditor with all data, documents, and other information, regardless of classification, that the legislative auditor requests to conduct an audit. The legislative auditor shall periodically report audit results and recommendations to the Legislative Audit Commission and the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over health and human services policy and finance.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, a "managed care organization" means a demonstration provider as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.69#stat.256B.69.2">256B.69, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.2c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2c.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.27.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 27</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.2d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2d.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.27.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 27</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Audit contracts.</span></h2>
          <p> A state department, board, commission, or other state agency contracting with a public accountant for an audit, except a contract negotiated by the state auditor for an audit of a local government, must provide the legislative auditor with a copy of the final report of the audit upon completion of the audit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.972.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/3/#laws.3.140.0">1Sp2019 c 3 art 3 s 140</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.972.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/18">3276</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1913/0/555/#laws.0.3.0">1913 c 555 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1949/0/33/#laws.0.2.0">1949 c 33 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.28.0">1973 c 492 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/317/#laws.0.2.0">1983 c 317 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/542/#laws.0.4.0">1992 c 542 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/109/#laws.2.1.0">2001 c 109 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/191/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 191 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.2.6.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.4.1.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 4 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/3/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp2019 c 3 art 3 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.5.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.22.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.973"><b>3.973</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/99/#laws.0.24.0">1999 c 99 s 24</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.9735"><b>3.9735</b> MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.2.27.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 2 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.974">
        <h1 class="shn">3.974 DISTRIBUTION OF WRITTEN REPORT.</h1>
        <p>For each audit, evaluation, or other review completed, the legislative auditor shall provide a written report to the organization or individual audited, evaluated, or reviewed; the Legislative Reference Library; and the Legislative Audit Commission.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.974.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/18">3286-10</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/431/#laws.4.3.0">1939 c 431 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.29.0">1973 c 492 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/99/#laws.0.3.0">1999 c 99 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.9741">
        <h1 class="shn">3.9741 COST OF CERTAIN AUDITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.9741.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan Commission.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the audit of the financial accounts and affairs of a commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.595">473.595</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.604">473.604</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.703">473.703</a>, the affected Metropolitan Commission is liable to the state for the total cost and expenses of the audit, including the salaries paid to the examiners while actually engaged in making the examination. The legislative auditor may bill the Metropolitan Commission either monthly or at the completion of the audit. All collections received for the audits must be deposited in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.9741.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Postsecondary Education Board.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislative auditor may enter into an interagency agreement with the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities to conduct financial audits, in addition to audits conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.972#stat.3.972.2">3.972, subdivision 2</a>. All payments received for audits requested by the board shall be deposited in the special revenue fund and appropriated to the legislative auditor to pay audit expenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.9741.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Legacy funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The outdoor heritage fund, the clean water fund, the parks and trails fund, and the arts and cultural heritage fund must each reimburse the general fund, in the manner prescribed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.127">16A.127</a>, for costs incurred by the legislative auditor in examining financial activities relating to each fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.9741.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Minnesota Sports Facilities Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the audit of the financial accounts and affairs of the Minnesota Sports Facilities Authority, the authority is liable to the state for the total cost and expenses of the audit, including the salaries paid to the examiners while actually engaged in making the examination. The legislative auditor may bill the authority either monthly or at the completion of the audit. All collections received for the audits must be deposited in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.9741.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote"> Data security account; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p> The data security account is created in the special revenue fund. Receipts credited to the account are annually appropriated to the legislative auditor for the purpose of oversight relating to security of data stored and transmitted by state systems, including to:</p>
          <p>(1) review and audit the audit reports of subscribers and requesters submitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.327#stat.168.327.6">168.327, subdivision 6</a>, including but not limited to assessing compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.12#stat.171.12.7b">171.12, subdivision 7b</a>, paragraph (d), and producing findings and opinions; and</p>
          <p>(2) review and audit driver records subscription services and bulk data practices of the Department of Public Safety, including identifying any deficiencies and making recommendations to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.9741.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/638/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 638 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/13/#laws.2.2.0">1993 c 13 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/2/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp1993 c 2 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.4.1.0">1995 c 212 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/254/#laws.1.37.0">1995 c 254 art 1 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.3.1.0">2010 c 361 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/1/1/#laws.14.1.0">1Sp2010 c 1 art 14 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/299/#laws.1.3.0">2012 c 299 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/293/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 293 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.4.1.0">2023 c 68 art 4 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.975">
        <h1 class="shn">3.975 DUTIES CONCERNING MISUSE OF PUBLIC MONEY OR OTHER RESOURCES.</h1>
        <p>If a legislative auditor's examination discloses that a state official or employee has used money for a purpose other than the purpose for which the money was appropriated or discloses any other misuse of public money or other public resources, the legislative auditor shall file a report with the Legislative Audit Commission, the attorney general, and the appropriate county attorney. The attorney general shall seek recovery of money and other resources as the evidence may warrant. The county attorney shall cause criminal proceedings to be instituted as the evidence may warrant.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.975.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/18">3286-11</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/431/#laws.4.4.0">1939 c 431 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.30.0">1973 c 492 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/99/#laws.0.4.0">1999 c 99 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.5.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.976"><b>3.976</b> MS 1978 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/6.74">6.74</a>] </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.977"><b>3.977</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/6.75">6.75</a>]
</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.978">
        <h1 class="shn">3.978 AUXILIARY POWERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.978.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Subpoena power.</span></h2>
          <p>In all matters relating to official duties, the legislative auditor has the powers possessed by courts of law to issue and have subpoenas served.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.978.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Inquiry and inspection power; duty to aid legislative auditor.</span></h2>
          <p>All public officials and their deputies and employees, and all corporations, firms, and individuals having business involving the receipt, disbursement, or custody of public funds shall at all times: (1) afford reasonable facilities for examinations by the legislative auditor; (2) provide returns and reports required by the legislative auditor; (3) attend and answer under oath the legislative auditor's lawful inquiries; (4) produce and exhibit all books, accounts, documents, data of any classification, and property that the legislative auditor requests to inspect; and (5) in all things cooperate with the legislative auditor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.978.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a person refuses or neglects to obey any lawful direction of the legislative auditor, a deputy or assistant, or withholds any information, book, record, paper or other document called for by the legislative auditor for the purpose of examination, after having been lawfully required by order or subpoena, upon application by the auditor, a judge of the district court in the county where the order or subpoena was made returnable shall compel obedience or punish disobedience as for contempt, as in the case of a similar order or subpoena issued by the court.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who swears falsely concerning any matter stated under oath is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.978.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/118/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 118 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/163/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 163 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.7.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.23.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.979">
        <h1 class="shn">3.979 DATA CLASSIFICATION AND DISCLOSURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.979.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Data practices.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislative auditor is subject to the Government Data Practices Act, chapter 13, and shall protect from unlawful disclosure data classified as not public. If data provided by the legislative auditor to the commission is disseminated by the commission or its members or agents in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.05#stat.13.05.4">13.05, subdivision 4</a>, the commission is subject to liability under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.08#stat.13.08.1">13.08</a>, subdivisions 1 and 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.979.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Access to data by commission members.</span></h2>
          <p>Members of the commission have access to not public data that is collected or used by the legislative auditor only as authorized by resolution of the commission. The commission may not authorize its members to have access to private or confidential data on individuals collected or used in connection with the collection of any tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.979.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Audit data.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Audit" as used in this subdivision means a financial audit, program evaluation, special review, investigation, or assessment of an allegation or report submitted to the legislative auditor.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding any other law, data relating to an audit are confidential or protected nonpublic until the final report of the audit has been released by the legislative auditor or the audit is no longer being actively pursued. Upon release of a final audit report by the legislative auditor, data relating to an audit are public except data otherwise classified as not public.</p>
          <p>(c) Unless the data are subject to a more restrictive classification by another law, upon the legislative auditor's decision to no longer actively pursue an audit without the release of a final audit report, data relating to an audit are private or nonpublic except for data: (1) relating to the audit's existence, status, and disposition; and (2) that document the work of the legislative auditor. For any such audit, data identifying individuals or nongovernmental entities are private or nonpublic.</p>
          <p>(d) Data related to an audit but not published in the audit report and that the legislative auditor reasonably believes will be used in litigation are confidential or protected nonpublic until the litigation has been completed or is no longer being actively pursued.</p>
          <p>(e) Data that could reasonably be used to determine the identity of an individual or entity supplying data for an audit are private or nonpublic if the data supplied were needed for an audit and would not have been provided to the legislative auditor without an assurance that the identity of the individual or entity would remain private or nonpublic, or the legislative auditor reasonably believes that the data would not have been provided.</p>
          <p>(f) Data related to an audit that were obtained from a nongovernmental entity have the classification that the data would have if obtained from the governmental entity for which the data were created, collected, or maintained by the nongovernmental entity.</p>
          <p>(g) The legislative auditor may disseminate data of any classification to:</p>
          <p>(1) a governmental entity, other than a law enforcement agency or prosecuting authority, if the dissemination of the data aids a pending audit; or</p>
          <p>(2) a law enforcement agency or prosecuting authority if there is reason to believe that the data are evidence of criminal activity within the agency's or authority's jurisdiction.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) Notwithstanding the classification of data as confidential or protected nonpublic, an individual or entity who supplies information for an audit may authorize the legislative auditor to release data that would identify the individual or entity for the purpose of conducting the audit. Data disseminated pursuant to this paragraph are subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.03#stat.13.03.4">13.03, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.979.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Review of data; data protection.</span></h2>
          <p>If, before releasing a report, the legislative auditor provides a person with data relating to the audit for the purpose of review and verification of the data, the person must protect the data from unlawful disclosure or be subject to the penalties and liabilities provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.08">13.08</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.09">13.09</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.979.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/262/#laws.0.2.0">2006 c 262 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.979.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions of terms provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02">13.02</a> apply for purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.979.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.12.0">1973 c 492 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/720/#laws.0.76.0">1973 c 720 s 76</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/204/#laws.0.90.0">1975 c 204 s 90</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/484/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 484 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/311/#laws.0.39.0">1981 c 311 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/545/#laws.0.24.0">1982 c 545 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/317/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 317 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/351/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 351 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.38.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/4/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 4 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.3.15.0">1994 c 632 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/184/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 184 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/99/#laws.0.23.0">1999 c 99 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.8.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.24.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 24</a>-26</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FISCAL NOTES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.98">
        <h1 class="shn">3.98 FISCAL NOTES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.98.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Preparation; duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The head or chief administrative officer of each department or agency of the state government, including the supreme court, shall prepare a fiscal note consistent with the standards and procedures adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.8853">3.8853</a>, at the request of the chair of the standing committee to which a bill has been referred, or the chair of the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee, or the chair of the senate Committee on Finance.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, "supreme court" includes all agencies, committees, and commissions supervised or appointed by the state supreme court or the state court administrator.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.98.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The fiscal note, where possible, shall:</p>
          <p>(1) cite the effect in dollar amounts;</p>
          <p>(2) cite the statutory provisions affected;</p>
          <p>(3) estimate the increase or decrease in revenues or expenditures;</p>
          <p>(4) include the costs which may be absorbed without additional funds;</p>
          <p>(5) include the assumptions used in determining the cost estimates; and</p>
          <p>(6) specify any long-range implication.</p>
          <p>(b) The fiscal note may comment on technical or mechanical defects in the bill but shall express no opinions concerning the merits of the proposal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.98.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Distribution.</span></h2>
          <p>A copy of the fiscal note shall be delivered to the chair of the Ways and Means Committee of the house of representatives, the chair of the Finance Committee of the senate, the chair of the standing committee to which the bill has been referred, to the chief author of the bill and to the commissioner of management and budget.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.98.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/214/#laws.5.16.0">2018 c 214 art 5 s 16</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/355/#laws.0.34.0">1974 c 355 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/793/#laws.0.33.0">1978 c 793 s 33</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.1.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/4/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 4 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.12.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/284/#laws.2.2.0">2004 c 284 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.2.7.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 2 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/214/#laws.5.7.0">2018 c 214 art 5 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.981"><b>3.981</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.1.13.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 1 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.982"><b>3.982</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.11.8.0">1997 c 231 art 11 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.983"><b>3.983</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.1.13.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 1 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.3.984"><b>3.984</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/629/#laws.0.6.0">1994 c 629 s 6</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.985">
        <h1 class="shn">3.985 RULE NOTES.</h1>
        <p>The governor or the chair of a standing committee to which a bill delegating rulemaking authority has been referred may require an agency to which the rulemaking authority is granted under a bill to prepare a rulemaking note on the proposed delegation of authority. The rulemaking note shall contain any of the following information requested by the governor or the chair of the standing committee: the reasons for the grant of authority; the person or groups the rules would impact; estimated cost of the rule for affected persons; estimated cost to the agency of adopting the rules; and any areas of controversy anticipated by the agency. The rulemaking note must be delivered to the governor and to the chair of the standing committee to which the bill delegating the rulemaking authority has been referred.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.985.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/629/#laws.0.4.0">1994 c 629 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LOCAL FISCAL IMPACTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.986">
        <h1 class="shn">3.986 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.986.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The terms used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.986">3.986</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.989">3.989</a> have the meanings given them in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.986.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local fiscal impact.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Local fiscal impact" means increased or decreased costs or revenues that a political subdivision would incur as a result of a law enacted after June 30, 1997, or rule proposed after December 31, 1999:</p>
          <p>(1) that mandates a new program, eliminates an existing mandated program, requires an increased level of service of an existing program, or permits a decreased level of service in an existing mandated program;</p>
          <p>(2) that implements or interprets federal law and, by its implementation or interpretation, increases or decreases program or service levels beyond the level required by the federal law;</p>
          <p>(3) that implements or interprets a statute or amendment adopted or enacted pursuant to the approval of a statewide ballot measure by the voters and, by its implementation or interpretation, increases or decreases program or service levels beyond the levels required by the ballot measure;</p>
          <p>(4) that removes an option previously available to political subdivisions, or adds an option previously unavailable to political subdivisions, thus requiring higher program or service levels or permitting lower program or service levels, or prohibits a specific activity and so forces political subdivisions to use a more costly alternative to provide a mandated program or service;</p>
          <p>(5) that requires that an existing program or service be provided in a shorter time period and thus increases the cost of the program or service, or permits an existing mandated program or service to be provided in a longer time period, thus permitting a decrease in the cost of the program or service;</p>
          <p>(6) that adds new requirements to an existing optional program or service and thus increases the cost of the program or service because the political subdivisions have no reasonable alternative other than to continue the optional program;</p>
          <p>(7) that affects local revenue collections by changes in property or sales and use tax exemptions;</p>
          <p>(8) that requires costs previously incurred at local option that have subsequently been mandated by the state; or</p>
          <p>(9) that requires payment of a new fee or increases the amount of an existing fee, or permits the elimination or decrease of an existing fee mandated by the state.</p>
          <p>(b) When state law is intended to achieve compliance with federal law or court orders, state mandates shall be determined as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) if the federal law or court order is discretionary, the state law is a state mandate;</p>
          <p>(2) if the state law exceeds what is required by the federal law or court order, only the provisions of the state law that exceed the federal requirements are a state mandate; and</p>
          <p>(3) if the state law does not exceed what is required by the federal statute or regulation or court order, the state law is not a state mandate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.986.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mandate.</span></h2>
          <p>A "mandate" is a requirement imposed upon a political subdivision in a law by a state agency or by judicial authority that, if not complied with, results in:</p>
          <p>(1) civil liability;</p>
          <p>(2) criminal penalty; or</p>
          <p>(3) administrative sanctions such as reduction or loss of funding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.986.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Political subdivision.</span></h2>
          <p>A "political subdivision" is a school district, county, or home rule charter or statutory city.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.986.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Requiring an increased level of service.</span></h2>
          <p>"Requiring an increased level of service" includes requiring that an existing service be provided in a shorter time.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.986.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.11.1.0">1997 c 231 art 11 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.16.1.0">1998 c 389 art 16 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.16.1.0">1999 c 243 art 16 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/21/#laws.11.1.0">1Sp2003 c 21 art 11 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.987">
        <h1 class="shn">3.987 LOCAL IMPACT NOTES FOR STATE-MANDATED ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.987.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Local impact notes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The Legislative Budget Office shall coordinate the development of a local impact note for any proposed legislation upon request of the chair or the ranking minority member of either legislative Tax, Finance, or Ways and Means Committee. Upon receipt of a request to prepare a local impact note, the office must notify the authors of the proposed legislation that the request has been made. The local impact note must be made available to the public upon request. If the action is among the exceptions listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.988">3.988</a>, a local impact note need not be requested nor prepared. The office shall make a reasonable and timely estimate of the local fiscal impact on each type of political subdivision that would result from the proposed legislation. The office may require any political subdivision or the commissioner of an administrative agency of the state to supply in a timely manner any information determined to be necessary to determine local fiscal impact. The political subdivision, its representative association, or commissioner shall convey the requested information to the office with a signed statement to the effect that the information is accurate and complete to the best of its ability. The political subdivision, its representative association, or commissioner, when requested, shall update its determination of local fiscal impact based on actual cost or revenue figures, improved estimates, or both. Upon completion of the note, the office must provide a copy to the authors of the proposed legislation and to the chair and ranking minority member of each committee to which the proposed legislation is referred.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.987.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Mandate explanations.</span></h2>
          <p>Before a committee hearing on a bill that seeks to impose program or financial mandates on political subdivisions, the chair or ranking minority member of the committee may request that the author must provide the committee with a note that gives appropriate responses to the following guidelines. The note must state and list:</p>
          <p>(1) the policy goals that are sought to be attained and any performance standards that are to be imposed on political subdivisions;</p>
          <p>(2) any performance standards that will allow political subdivisions flexibility and innovation of method in achieving those goals;</p>
          <p>(3) the process by which each standard governs input such as staffing and other administrative aspects of the program;</p>
          <p>(4) the sources of additional revenue, in addition to existing funding for similar programs, that are directly linked to imposition of the mandates that will provide adequate and stable funding for their requirements;</p>
          <p>(5) the reasons why financial incentives or voluntary compliance would not yield the equity, efficiency, or desired level of statewide uniformity in the proposed program;</p>
          <p>(6) what input has been obtained to ensure that the implementing agencies have the capacity to carry out the delegated responsibilities; and</p>
          <p>(7) the efforts put forth, if any, to involve political subdivisions in the creation or development of the proposed mandate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.987.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.16.36.0">1998 c 389 art 16 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.987.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">No mandate restriction.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as specifically provided by this article, nothing in this article restricts or eliminates the authority of the state to create or impose programs by law upon political subdivisions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.987.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.11.2.0">1997 c 231 art 11 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/300/#laws.3.1.0">1998 c 300 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.16.3.0">1998 c 389 art 16 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.16.2.0">1999 c 243 art 16 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.16.1.0">2008 c 154 art 16 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/306/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 306 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/214/#laws.5.12.0">2018 c 214 art 5 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.988">
        <h1 class="shn">3.988 EXCEPTIONS TO LOCAL IMPACT NOTES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.988.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Costs resulting from inflation.</span></h2>
          <p>A local impact note need not be prepared for increases in the cost of providing an existing service if the increases result directly from inflation. "Resulting directly from inflation" means attributable to maintaining an existing level of service rather than increasing the level of service. A cost-of-living increase in welfare benefits is an example of a cost resulting directly from inflation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.988.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Costs not result of new program or increased service.</span></h2>
          <p>A local impact note need not be prepared for increased local costs that do not result from a new program or an increased level of service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.988.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Miscellaneous exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A local impact note or an attachment as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.987#stat.3.987.2">3.987, subdivision 2</a>, need not be prepared for the cost of a mandated action if the law containing the mandate:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) accommodates a specific local request;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) results in no new local government duties;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) leads to revenue losses from exemptions to taxes;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) provided only clarifying or conforming, nonsubstantive charges on local government;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) imposes additional net local costs that are minor (an amount less than or equal to one-half of one percent of the local revenue base as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.011#stat.477A.011.27">477A.011, subdivision 27</a>, or $50,000, whichever is less for any single local government if the mandate does not apply statewide or less than $1,000,000 if the mandate is statewide);</p>
          <p class="in">(6) is a law or executive order enacted before July 1, 1997, or a rule initially implementing a law enacted before July 1, 1997;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) implements something other than a law or executive order, such as a federal, court, or voter-approved mandate;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) results in savings that equal or exceed costs;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) requires the holding of elections;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) ensures due process or equal protection;</p>
          <p class="in">(11) provides for the notification and conduct of public meetings;</p>
          <p class="in">(12) establishes the procedures for administrative and judicial review of actions taken by political subdivisions;</p>
          <p class="in">(13) protects the public from malfeasance, misfeasance, or nonfeasance by officials of political subdivisions;</p>
          <p class="in">(14) relates directly to financial administration, including the levy, assessment, and collection of taxes;</p>
          <p class="in">(15) relates directly to the preparation and submission of financial audits necessary to the administration of state laws; or</p>
          <p class="in">(16) requires uniform standards to apply to public and private institutions without differentiation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.988.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.11.3.0">1997 c 231 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.16.5.0">1998 c 389 art 16 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.16.2.0">2008 c 154 art 16 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.3.989">
        <h1 class="shn">3.989 REIMBURSEMENT TO LOCAL POLITICAL SUBDIVISIONS FOR COSTS OF STATE MANDATES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.989.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>In this section:</p>
          <p>(1) "Class A state mandates" means those laws under which the state mandates to political subdivisions, their participation, the organizational structure of the program, and the procedural regulations under which the law must be administered; and</p>
          <p>(2) "Class B state mandates" means those mandates resulting from legislation enacted after July 1, 1998, that specifically reference this section and that allow the political subdivisions to opt for administration of a law with program elements mandated beforehand and with an assured revenue level from the state of at least 90 percent of full program and administrative costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.989.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compilation of local impact notes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of management and budget shall post to the agency website a copy of all local impact notes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.989.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certain political subdivisions; report.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The political subdivisions that have opted to administer class B state mandates shall report to the commissioner of management and budget by September 1, 1998, and by September 1 of each year thereafter, identifying each instance when revenue for a class B state mandate has fallen below 85 percent of the total cost of the program and the political subdivision intends to cease administration of the program.</p>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall forward a copy of the report to the chairs of the appropriate funding committees of the senate and the house of representatives.</p>
          <p class="in">The political subdivision may exercise its option to cease administration only if the legislature has failed to include the shortfall as an appropriation in the state budget for the next fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.3.989.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Laws and executive orders enumerated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.988">3.988</a> are exempted from this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.3.989.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.11.4.0">1997 c 231 art 11 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.16.6.0">1998 c 389 art 16 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.16.3.0">2008 c 154 art 16 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.6.0">2013 c 134 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
    </div>
